V_{m}\;\mp\;c_{r}^{3}a^{3-h}\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\pi^{3}}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\Upsilon_{k}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot V_{j}=i\,k\,H^{Inc}(z_{m})+{\mathcal{O}}(kd)+\widetilde{\mathrm{Error}}_{m},

where
\xi
is given by
(\ref{def-xi})
and

V_{m}\;:=\;\frac{\pi^{3}}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(x)\,dx,\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leq m\leq\aleph.

Moreover, there holds the following
\ell_{2}
-estimates,

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|\widetilde{\mathrm{Error}}_{m}\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\frac{\xi^{2}}{\left|\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right|^{2}}\,\,\left([H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{2\,\alpha-3}\,\left|\log d\right|^{2}+\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}\right),

and

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|V_{m}\;-\;U_{m}\right|^{2}\lesssim\frac{\left|\xi\right|^{2}}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-12\,\xi\right|^{2}\;\left|\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right|^{2}}\;\left([H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}d^{2\,\alpha\,-\,3}\,\left|\log d\right|^{2}\,+\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}\right).

The proof of
Proposition
3.2
is very similar to the proof of
Proposition
2.3
. Indeed, from the proof of
Proposition
2.3
presented in
Section
4
, we notice that the main methods utilized therein are independent of the precise form of the effective permeability
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
. Therefore, with the help of
Proposition
3.1
, which shows the
C^{0,\alpha}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
-regularity of the solution
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
, we obtain the a-priori estimate results directly by substituting the explicit representations of the constant tensors
{\bf P_{0}}
, given by (
3.4
), and
{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
, given by (
3.8
). To avoid being redundant, we omit the detailed proof here.

3.4.
Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

We divide the proof into the following few parts
to demonstrate the estimation of the far field collected by exciting a medium having a negative permeability. The initial step is dedicated to demonstrating that
{\bf I}\,-\,\dfrac{12\,\xi}{\pi^{3}+4\xi}\,\nabla{\bf M}
is the dominant operator in the L.S.E given by
(\ref{LS-1})
.
The second step involves computing low-frequency resonances that are related to the L.S.E given by
(\ref{LS-1})
. We will prove that they are small and related to the dominant operator given by
{\bf I}\,-\,\dfrac{12\,\xi}{\pi^{3}+4\xi}\,\nabla{\bf M}
. The field generated at the previously computed low-frequency plasmonic resonances is estimated in the third part. In the fourth part, we finish estimating the far field that was generated. We conclude the fourth part by mentioning a valuable remark regarding the effective permeability tensor expression and the far field expression which depends on the dominated operator’s size in
(\ref{LS-1})
. In the last part, we discuss a particular case when the domain
\Omega
is a ball and the excitation of the first eigenvalue related to the Magnetization operator defined on a ball.

We start by recalling the L.S.E given by
(\ref{LS-1})
,

(3.10)

\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}-{\bf K}\right)H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\,=\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}(x,\theta),

where the parameter
\xi
is given by
(\ref{def-xi})
and the operator
{\bf K}
is defined by

(3.11)

\displaystyle{\bf K}(F)\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\pm\,\xi\,\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot F\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{exp-T-neg})}{=}

\displaystyle\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(F\right).

To investigate
(\ref{LS-vec})
, we first show that
{\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
dominates the operator
{\bf K}
. Thanks to the Helmholtz decomposition of
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, given by
(\ref{hel-decomp})
, we have

\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)=\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)\right)+\overset{2}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)\right)+\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)\right).

Next, we derive sufficient conditions such that

(3.12)

\left\|\overset{j}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\,\gg\,\left\|{\bf K}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))},\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,2,3.

To do this, we first recall the Helmholtz decomposition
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)=\mathbb{H}_{0}(\mathrm{div}\,=0)\overset{\perp}{\otimes}\mathbb{H}_{0}(Curl=0)\overset{\perp}{\otimes}\nabla\mathcal{H}armonic
to which we correspond a basis given by three subfamilies
(e^{1}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}},(e^{2}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(e^{3}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
where the first two sequences are basis of the first two subspaces generated by the projection, on them, of the vector Newtonian operator while the third sequence is a basis of the third space generated by the Magnetization operator
\nabla{\bf M}
, see
[
15
]
. We use the fact that

(3.13)

\nabla{\bf M}(e_{n}^{(1)})=0,\nabla{\bf M}(e_{n}^{(2)})=e_{n}^{(2)}\mbox{ and }\nabla{\bf M}(e_{n}^{(3)})=\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\;e_{n}^{(3)},\;\forall\,n,

to derive the following estimation

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\cdot H\right)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}\left|\langle\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\cdot H,e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}

(3.14)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}\left|\langle H,e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\pm\,\langle\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(H),e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle\right|^{2}=\sum_{n}\left|\langle H,e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2},

invoking the fact that
\nabla{\bf M}
is self-adjoint. Again by
(\ref{M-prop})
, we have

\langle\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(2)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,=\,\langle\xi{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},\nabla{\bf M}(e_{n}^{(2)})\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,=\,\langle\xi{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(2)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

and then,

\left\lVert\overset{2}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=\sum_{n}\left|\langle\left({\bf I}\pm\xi{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H),e_{n}^{(2)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}\overset{\textnormal{(\ref{mu-r0})}}{=}\sum_{n}\left|\langle\mathring{\mu_{r}}\cdot H,e_{n}^{(2)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2},

with

(3.15)

\mathring{\mu_{r}}:={\bf I}\pm\xi{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\,\overset{(\ref{mu-r0-diag})}{=}\,\frac{\left(\pi^{3}\pm 8\,\xi\right)}{\left(\pi^{3}\mp 4\,\xi\right)}\,{\bf I},

which gives us a representation of
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
in the form proportional to the identity. Thus we have

(3.16)

\left\lVert\overset{2}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=\left|\mathring{\mu_{r}}\right|^{2}\,\sum_{n}\left|\langle H,e_{n}^{(2)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}.

Similarly, by using the relation that

\left\langle\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(H),e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\left\langle\xi{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(H),\nabla{\bf M}(e_{n}^{(3)})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\;=\;\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\;\left\langle\xi{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(H),e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

we obtain

\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\cdot H\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=\sum_{n}\left|\left\langle\left({\bf I}\pm\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\cdot H,e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}=\sum_{n}\left|\left\langle A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}\cdot H,e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2},

with

(3.17)

A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}\,:=\,{\bf I}\,\pm\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\,\overset{(\ref{exp-T-neg})}{=}\left(1\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)}{\pi^{3}}\left(1\,\mp\frac{4\xi}{\pi^{3}}\right)^{-1}\right)\,{\bf I}.

Similar to
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
, since the tensor
A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}
is proportional to the identity, we obtain

(3.18)

\left\lVert\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\cdot H\right)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=\sum_{n}\left|A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}\right|^{2}\,\left|\left\langle H,e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}\,\geq\,\left|A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right|^{2}\sum_{n}\,\left|\left\langle H,e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2},

where

\left|A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right|:=\underset{n}{Inf}\left|A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}\right|.

Then, by gathering
(\ref{P1-h})
,
(\ref{P2-hm})
and
(\ref{P3-hm})
, we deduce that

\left\lVert\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left\lVert\overset{i}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(H)\right)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\geq\min\left\{1,\lvert\mathring{\mu_{r}}\rvert^{2},\lvert A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\right\}\lVert H\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

which further indicates that the operator norm fulfills

(3.19)

\left\lVert{\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}\geq\min\left\{1,\lvert\mathring{\mu_{r}}\rvert,\lvert A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\right\}.

Now, we evaluate the operator norm of
{\bf K}
. To achieve this, recall from
(\ref{TD09})
that

{\bf K}(F)\,=\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(F\right),

and, thanks to
[
15
, formulas (2.8) & (2.9)]
, we rewrite the R.H.S as

(3.20)

\displaystyle{\bf K}(F)(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\Bigg[\frac{k^{2}}{2}\,N(F)+\frac{k^{2}}{2}\,N^{\prime}(F)+\frac{ik^{3}}{6\pi}\,\int_{\Omega}F(y)\,dy

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{4\pi}\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{(ik)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!}\int_{\Omega}\lVert x-z\rVert^{n}\,F(z)\,dz+\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{n\geq 3}\frac{(ik)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\nabla\left(\lVert x-z\rVert^{n}\right)\cdot F(z)\,dz\,\Bigg],

where
N^{\prime}(\cdot)
is the operator defined by
(\ref{DefNprimeTHM})
. Now, by taking the
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)-
norm on the both sides of (
3.20
) and using the fact that the second term on the R.H.S. can be evaluated in a same way as the first one, we obtain that

(3.21)

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf K}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{12\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right|}\,\Bigg[k^{2}\,\left\|N\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}+\frac{k^{3}}{6\,\pi}\,\left|\Omega\right|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}\,\left|\Omega\right|}{4\,\pi\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)}\,\sum_{n\geq 2}\frac{\left(k\;\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)\right)^{n}}{n!}\,+\frac{k^{3}\,\left|\Omega\right|}{16\,\pi}\,\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\left(k\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)\right)^{n}}{n\,!}\,\,\Bigg].

Moreover, in
[
15
, Section 5.1]
, we have justified the convergence of the last two series appearing on the R.H.S of (
3.21
). By referring to
[
33
, Theorem 3.3]
, we have

\lVert N\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq\lVert N\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\textbf{B});\mathbb{L}^{2}(\textbf{B}))}\leq\left(\frac{3}{4\,\pi}\,\left|\textbf{B}\right|\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\lVert N\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(B(0,1));\mathbb{L}^{2}(B(0,1)))},

where
B
is a ball such that
\left|\Omega\right|=\left|\textbf{B}\right|
, and the last inequality is derived from the Newtonian operator’s scale. In addition, due to
[
9
, Corollary 4.2]
, we have

\lVert N\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(B(0,1));\mathbb{L}^{2}(B(0,1)))}=\frac{4}{\pi^{2}}.

Then, by the use of
\left|\Omega\right|=\left|\textbf{B}\right|
and
\left|\Omega\right|>1
, we end up with the following estimation

\lVert N\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\lesssim\frac{\left|\Omega\right|}{\pi}.

This implies,

(3.22)

\lVert{\bf K}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq\frac{12\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right|}\frac{\left|\Omega\right|}{\pi}\;c(k).

where
c(\cdot)
is the frequency function given by

c(k):=k^{2}\,+\frac{k^{3}}{6}\,+\frac{k^{2}}{4\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)}\,\sum_{n\geq 2}\frac{\left(k\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)\right)^{n}}{n!}+\frac{k^{3}}{16}\,\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\left(k\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)\right)^{n}}{n!}.

By combining
(\ref{low-bd-finalm})
with
(\ref{es-Km})
, we derive

(3.23)

\frac{12\;\xi\;c(k)}{\pi\,\left|\pi^{3}\mp 4\,\xi\right|}\,\left|\Omega\right|\,<\,\min\left\{1,\lvert\mathring{\mu_{r}}\rvert,\lvert A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\right\}.

This gives us a sufficient condition ensuring
(\ref{Scdt})
, and ends the first part of the proof.

Now, even if the tensors
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
and
A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
are both characterized by a chosen sign, which allows us to distinguish two cases, we will focus on the sequel only on the lower sign. Taking the lower sign, using the formulas
(\ref{Tmur})
and
(\ref{A-mu})
, the inequality
(\ref{compa-bdm})
becomes

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\;\frac{8\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}};\;\;\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\right\},

which under the condition
\xi>\,\dfrac{\pi^{3}}{2}
, which is satisfied for
\xi>2\,\delta\,\pi^{3}
, with
\delta\geq 1
, can be further reformulated as,

(3.24)

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\;\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\right\},

where we shall provide more details on the real parameter
\delta
later. To investigate the more interesting case that

\min\left\{1;\;\;\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\right\}=\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\neq 0,

we should exclude the following sequence of frequencies
k
such that,

(3.25)

\xi_{n}=\frac{\pi^{3}}{4\,\left(3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}.

By a straightforward computation, we know that

(3.26)

\xi_{m_{0}}:=\frac{\pi^{3}}{4\left(3\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)-1\right)}

is the solution to the (dispersion) equation

\pi^{3}+4\xi\left(1-3\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\right)=0.

In addition, the sequence

\{\xi_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\;\in\;]2\,\delta\,\pi^{3},\infty[,

where we recall that the lower bound, in the previous interval, is the one fixed for
\xi
.
To avoid
(\ref{eq-main-ineq})
being meaningless (its R.H.S is zero), we introduce a small parameter
\beta
, i.e.
\left|\beta\right|\ll 1
and
\left|\beta\right|\ll\xi_{m_{0}}
, such that
6
6

6

The parameter
\beta
should be chosen such that
\xi
, given by
(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})
, satisfies
\xi\neq\xi_{n}
,
\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\{\xi_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is the sequence given by
(\ref{ExcSeqxn})
. In addition, it’s multiplication by the constant
\dfrac{\pi^{3}}{4}
is only for technical reasons.
in (
3.24
),

(3.27)

\xi=\xi_{m_{0}}+\frac{\pi^{3}}{4}\,\beta=\frac{\pi^{3}}{4}\;\left[\frac{1}{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}+\beta\right].

Next, by using
\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right);\,\xi_{m_{0}}\right)
, we get

(3.28)

\displaystyle\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\;\;

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})}{=}

\displaystyle\;\frac{\left|\beta\right|\;\left|3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right|^{2}}{\left|3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)+\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right)\right|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\beta\right|\right).

In the sequel, we denote by
f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right)
the dominant part of the R.H.S of the previous inequality, i.e.

(3.29)

f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right)\;:=\;\frac{\left|\beta\right|\;\left|3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right|^{2}}{3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)}

Moreover, we have

(3.30)

f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\beta\right|\right)\,\ll\,1.

Then, by combining with
(\ref{eq-main-ineq})
and
(\ref{Inff})
, near the resonances, we derive that

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)\,\left|\Omega\right|}{\pi\,\left(\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi\right)}\,<\,f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right),

which yields

(3.31)

c(k)\;<\;\frac{\pi\,\left(\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi\right)}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}\;f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right)\overset{(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})}{=}\;\left[\frac{\pi\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)}{\left|\Omega\right|}\,+\,g\left(\beta\right)\right]\;f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right),

where

(3.32)

g\left(\beta\right):=\frac{\pi\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)}{\left|\Omega\right|}\;\beta\;\frac{\left(2\,-\,3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,\dfrac{1}{3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\right)}{1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)},\quad\text{with}\quad g\left(\beta\right)\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\beta\right|\right).

Now, by recalling the definition of
\xi
, given by
(\ref{def-xi})
, and using the expression of
\xi
given by
(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})
, we obtain

(3.33)

k^{2}\,=\,\frac{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}{\eta_{0}}\;\frac{\pi^{3}}{4}\;\left[\frac{1}{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}\,+\,\beta\right].

Moreover, since
k
fulfills the condition given by
(\ref{condition-on-k})
, which implies that

(3.34)

k^{2}\,=\,\frac{1\,\mp\,c_{0}\,a^{h}}{\eta\,a^{2}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}\left(B\right)}\,\overset{(\ref{contrast-epsilon})}{=}\,\frac{1}{\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)}\,\mp\,\frac{c_{0}\,a^{h}}{\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)},

and, by gathering
(\ref{exp-k-p})
with
(\ref{exp-k-d})
, we end up with the following relation

\frac{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}{\eta_{0}}\,\frac{\pi^{3}}{4}\,\left[\frac{1}{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}\,+\,\beta\,\right]\,=\,\frac{1}{\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}\left(B\right)}\mp\frac{c_{0}\,a^{h}}{\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}\left(B\right)}.

This implies,

c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,=\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\left[\frac{1}{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}\,+\,\beta\right]^{-1}\;\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)}\,\mp\,\frac{c_{0}\,a^{h}}{\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)}\right),

which can be reduced, using the smallness of
\beta
and the parameter
a
, to

(3.35)

c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,=\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\;\frac{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}{\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)}\,+\mathcal{O}\left(a^{h}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(\beta\right).

By keeping the dominant term
of
\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,
, given in
(\ref{eq-cocr})
, and substituting it into
(\ref{exp-k-p})
, we obtain

(3.36)

k^{2}\,=\,\frac{1}{\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)}\,+\,\beta\;\frac{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)}{\eta_{0}\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)}.

Remember that
k
is the argument of the frequency function
c(\cdot)
defined by
(\ref{def-ck})
and satisfying
(\ref{cond-ck2})
. Then, we can get from (
3.31
) that

(3.37)

k^{2}\,+\frac{k^{3}}{6}\,+\frac{k^{2}}{4\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)}\,\sum_{n\geq 2}\frac{\left(k\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)\right)^{n}}{n!}+\frac{k^{3}}{16}\,\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\left(k\,\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)\right)^{n}}{n!}\;<\;\left[\frac{\pi\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)}{\left|\Omega\right|}\,+\,g\left(\beta\right)\right]\;f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta\right),

and, as all the terms on the L.H.S of (
3.37
) are positive, we deduce that

(3.38)

k^{2}\;<\;\left[\frac{\pi\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)}{\left|\Omega\right|}\,+\,g\left(\beta\right)\right]\;f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right)\,\underset{(\ref{small-f})}{\overset{(\ref{Deffctg})}{\lesssim}}\,\left|\beta\right|\,\ll\,1.

This implies the smallness of the frequency
k
. Indeed, if
\eta_{0}
is large enough such that the inequality
(\ref{c(.)Series})
holds, then the frequency
k
generated by
(\ref{kfreqplasmon})
can induce a plasmonic resonances. This concludes the second part of the proof.

Next, to estimate the field generated incident frequencies
k
satisfying (
3.36
), we need to estimate the Fourier coefficients related to the magnetic field
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
, i.e.
\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(j)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
, with
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
j=1,2,3
. To do this, we start by rewriting the L.S.E given by
(\ref{LS-vec})
as

(3.39)

\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}={\bf K}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}+ikH^{Inc}(x,\theta).

By repeating the calculations as before, it is obvious that the term on the L.H.S of (
3.39
) can be written as follows

\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M^{0}}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}=\sum_{n}\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}e_{n}^{(1)}+\sum_{n}\left\langle\mathring{\mu_{r}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(2)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}e_{n}^{(2)}+\sum_{n}\left\langle A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}e_{n}^{(3)},

where
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
is the tensor given by
(\ref{Tmur})
and
A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
is the tensor defined by
(\ref{A-mu})
. In addition, since
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
and
(\ref{Tmur})
and
A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
are proportional to the identity matrix, we get from the previous formula that

(3.40)

\displaystyle\left({\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M^{0}}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{n}\,\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}e_{n}^{(1)}\,+\,\sum_{n}\,\mathring{\mu_{r}}\,\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(2)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}e_{n}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\,A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}\,\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}e_{n}^{(3)}.

Similarly, by projecting the R.H.S of (
3.39
) onto the three sub-spaces, we can obtain that

\displaystyle{\bf K}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}+ikH^{Inc}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\,\sum_{n}\,\left\langle{\bf K}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}+ikH^{Inc},e_{n}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(j)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{TD09})}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\,\sum_{n}\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\left\langle\,\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(j)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\,\sum_{n}\,ik\,\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(j)}.

Knowing that
\nabla{\bf M^{k}}
, and also
\nabla{\bf M}
, when restricted to
\mathbb{H}_{0}(\mathrm{div}\,=0)
is a vanishing operator, we get from the previous formula

\displaystyle{\bf K}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}+ikH^{Inc}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,k^{2}\,\left\langle{\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{3}\,\sum_{n}\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\left\langle\,\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(j)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\,\sum_{n}\,ik\,\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(j)}.

And, regarding the subspace
\mathbb{H}_{0}\left(Curl=0\right)
, we have

\nabla{\bf M^{-k}}=\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{-k}}+\textbf{I}\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla{\bf M}=\textbf{I}.

This implies,

(3.41)

\displaystyle{\bf K}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}+ikH^{Inc}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,k^{2}\,\left\langle{\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\,\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\left\langle\,\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(3)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\,\sum_{n}\,ik\,\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,e_{n}^{(j)}.

By combining with (
3.40
) and (
3.41
), together with the expressions of
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
and
A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
, we get

(3.42)

\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=i\,k\,\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\;\pm\;\frac{12\,\xi\,k^{2}}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,\left\langle{\bf N}^{k}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

By projecting onto the second subspace we obtain

\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(2)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\frac{i\,k}{|\mathring{\mu_{r}}|}\;\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(2)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Since
\mathrm{div}\,\left(H^{Inc}\right)\,=\,0
, we get
\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(2)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=0
. Hence,

(3.43)

\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(2)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=0.

Finally, by projecting onto the third subspace,

(3.44)

\displaystyle\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i\,k\,\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\dfrac{\pm 12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,\langle\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}{|A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}},n}|}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{A-mu})}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{i\,k\,\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,}{\left(1\pm\dfrac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1\,\mp\,\dfrac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\dfrac{\pm 12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,\mp\,4\,\xi\right)}\,\langle\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}{\left(1\pm\dfrac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1\,\mp\,\dfrac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right)}.

Now, let
\xi
be close to
\xi_{m_{0}}
, as in
(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})
, then we have

(3.45)

\left|1\,+\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\right|\,=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad\forall\;n\in\mathbb{N},

and

(3.46)

\left|1\,-\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\right|\,=\,\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}\left(1\right),&\text{if $n\neq m_{0}$}\\
&\\
\mathcal{O}\left(f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta\right)\right),\;&\text{if $n=m_{0}$}\end{cases},

where
f\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right),\beta\right)
fulfills (
3.30
). This explains why we picked the lower sign instead of the upper sign. Hence, from the estimation of the Fourier coefficients, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\sum_{n}\left|\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}\;+\;k^{4}\,\sum_{n}\left|\left\langle{\bf N}^{k}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left|1-\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1+\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right|^{-2}\;k^{2}\,\sum_{n}\left|\left\langle H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}\,

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left|1-\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1+\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right|^{-2}\;\,\sum_{n}\left|\langle\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{n}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}.

For the second term on the R.H.S, we use the smallness of
k
and the boundedness of
\left\|{\bf N}^{k}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}
to move it into the L.H.S. In addition, the third term dominate the first one. With all these concerns, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\left(k^{2}\,\lVert H^{Inc}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\lVert\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\right]\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}{\left|1-\dfrac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1+\dfrac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{TD09})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\frac{\left(k^{2}\,\lVert H^{Inc}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\lVert{\bf K}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}{\left|1-\dfrac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1+\dfrac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right|^{2}}.

By using
(\ref{compa-bdm})
, we derive

\frac{\lVert{\bf K}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}}{\left|1-\dfrac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1+\dfrac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right|^{2}}\;\lesssim\;1,

which can be used to obtain

(3.47)

\left\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim\,\left|1-\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\xi\,\left(1+\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\xi\right)^{-1}\right|^{-1}\;k\;\lVert H^{Inc}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

This ends the proof of the third part. Additionally, since in
Theorem
1.4
, second point, the error term defined by
(\ref{es-error-neg})
, depends on both
\left\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}
and
[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}
, studying their reliance on the model parameters
k
and
\beta
is necessary. The objective of the upcoming lemma is to achieve this.

Proposition 3.3
.

We assume here that
\Omega
is a
C^{4,\alpha}
-regularity bounded domain.
We have the following estimations,

(3.48)

\left[H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right]_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\;\lesssim\;\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}\;=\;\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\right),

and

\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\;\lesssim\;[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})},

where
0\,<\,\alpha\,<\,1
and
k^{2}\lesssim\beta\ll 1
.

Proof.

The second point is a straightforward one. For the first one we refer the readers to
Subsection
5.2
.
∎

The estimation
(\ref{ToproveinAppendix})
proves that
[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}
is of order one with respect to the parameter
a
. Regarding the error term in
(\ref{es-error-neg})
, thanks to
Proposition
3.3
, we are able to deduce that

(3.49)

E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})-E^{\infty}(\hat{x})={\mathcal{O}}\left(c(k,\eta_{0},c_{r},c_{0})\;g_{c_{r}}(a)\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(a^{\frac{h}{3}}\right),

where

\displaystyle c(k,\eta_{0},c_{r},c_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{3}\,\eta_{0}^{2}\,c_{r}^{-3}}{c_{0}\left(c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,k^{2}\,\eta_{0}\right)}\left|\left(1\,\mp\,\frac{4\,\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pi^{3}\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\right)^{-1}\right|\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}

\displaystyle g_{c_{r}}(a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(c_{r}^{2\,\alpha}\,a^{2\,\alpha\,\left(1-\frac{h}{3}\right)}\,\left|\log\left(a\right)\right|^{2}\,+\,c_{r}^{\frac{12}{7}}\,a^{\frac{12}{7}\left(1-\frac{h}{3}\right)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\mbox{with}\quad\alpha\,\in(0,1).

The formula
(\ref{add-coro-4})
suggests that the far field
E^{\infty}(\hat{x})
is a small perturbation of the far field
E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})
.
Moreover, we recall that we have

(3.51)

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}\left(\hat{x}\right)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{exp-T-neg})}{=}

\displaystyle\,-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,\mp\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(z)\,dz.

Suggested by
(\ref{IBA})
and
(\ref{Equa0758})
, we choose the lower sign for
{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
, and we get

(3.52)

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}\left(\hat{x}\right)\,

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{Proj2=0})}{=}

\displaystyle-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\underbrace{\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(z)\,dz}_{I_{1,2}}.

To estimate
I_{1,2}
, we use the fact that
k
is a small parameter and by Taylor expansion of the function
e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}
, we get

(3.53)

e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,=\,1\,+\,\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{(-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z)^{n}}{n!},

and knowing that

\int_{\Omega}e_{n}^{(1)}(x)\,dx\,=\,0,\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N},

we deduce that the dominant part of
I_{1,2}
behaves as

\displaystyle I_{1,2}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{-\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z\,\hat{x}\,\times\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\left|I_{1,2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\;\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\overset{(\ref{Equa426})}{\lesssim}\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\;k\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}\,H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{6}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\right).

This implies,

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}\left(\hat{x}\right)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(z)\,dz+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{6}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\underbrace{\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\sum_{n\neq m_{0}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,e_{n}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}_{I_{n\neq m_{0}}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{6}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\right).

We estimate the series
I_{n\neq m_{0}}
, which gives us

\left|I_{n\neq m_{0}}\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\,\left(\sum_{n\neq m_{0}}\,\left|\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,e_{n}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\;\;\left(\sum_{n\neq m_{0}}\left|\langle\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{n}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

It is obvious to see from (
3.44
) that the
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
-norm of
\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
is bounded by the
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
-norm of
k\,H^{Inc}
, when
k
is away from the resonance frequency, which is of order
\mathcal{O}\left(k\right)
. Then we can derive the coming estimation

\left|I_{n\neq m_{0}}\right|\;\lesssim\;\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\,\left(\sum_{n\neq m_{0}}\,\left|\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-\,i\,k\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,e_{n}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\right).

Then, by utilizing the fact that
k
is small, we end up with:

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\,e^{-ik\hat{x}\cdot z}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{Texpk})}{=}

\displaystyle-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\int_{\Omega}\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\left(-ik\hat{x}\cdot z\right)^{j}}{j!}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\right).

Next, we estimate the second term on the R.H.S, as

\displaystyle\left|\cdots\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\int_{\Omega}\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{\left(-ik\hat{x}\cdot z\right)^{j}}{j!}\,e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\;\left|\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\overset{(\ref{comp-coro})}{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{5}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,\left|\beta\right|}\right).

This implies,

(3.54)

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{i\,k^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{\pm\,c_{0}}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,\left(1\,+\,\frac{4}{\pi^{3}}\,\xi\right)^{-1}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{5}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,\left|\beta\right|}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}\,\eta_{0}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}}\right).

The estimation of
E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})
is delayed until we obtain the dominant term associated to the Fourier coefficient
\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
. To achieve this, we observe from (
3.44
) that the projection of
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
onto the third subspace dominates and this gives us

(3.55)

\displaystyle\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{i\,k}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{i\,k}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}_{I_{\beta,1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\underbrace{\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}{\bf K}\left(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}_{I_{\beta,2}}.

Now, we estimate
I_{\beta,1}
and
I_{\beta,2}
appearing on the R.H.S of the previous formula.

(1)

Estimation of
I_{\beta,1}
.

\left|I_{\beta,1}\right|\lesssim\frac{k}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\,\left|\sum_{n}\left\langle\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}e_{n}^{(1)},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|.

Regarding the dominant term of
\left({\bf I}-\dfrac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}e_{n}^{(1)}
, we have the following approximation,

\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}e_{n}^{(1)}\,=\,e_{n}^{(1)}\,+\,\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\,\frac{12}{\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi}\,k^{4}\,\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\int_{\Omega}\left|\cdot-y\right|e_{n}^{(1)}(y)\,dy,

which implies, by taking the orthogonality fact between
e_{n}^{(1)}
and
e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}
,

\left|I_{\beta,1}\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{5}}{f^{2}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\,\left|\sum_{n}\left\langle\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\int_{\Omega}\left|\cdot-y\right|e_{n}^{(1)}(y)\,dy,e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|.

Successively, taking the adjoint operator of
\left({\bf I}-\dfrac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}
, using the self-adjointness of
\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}
and the adjoint operator related to the operator
V(\cdot)\,\rightarrow\,\int_{\Omega}\left|\cdot-y\right|\,V(y)\,dy
, we end up with the coming estimation

\left|I_{\beta,1}\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{5}}{f^{2}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\,\left|\sum_{n}\left\langle\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{Inc},e_{n}^{(1)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\left\langle e_{n}^{(1)},\int_{\Omega}\left|\cdot-y\right|\,\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\bf I}-\frac{\left(\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}\right)^{\star}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(y)\,dy\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|.

As the vector field
\int_{\Omega}\left|\cdot-y\right|\,\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\bf I}-\frac{\left(\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}\right)^{\star}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(y)\,dy
is moderate one, with respect to the frequency
k
, we end up with the following estimate

\displaystyle\left|I_{\beta,1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{k^{5}}{f^{2}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim\frac{k^{6}}{f^{2}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\overset{(\ref{small-f})}{=}\,\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{6}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\right).

(2)

Estimation of
I_{\beta,2}
.

\displaystyle\left|I_{\beta,2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}{\bf K}\left(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right),e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{small-f})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\left|\beta\right|^{-1}\,\left\|\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\,\left\|{\bf K}\left(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

and, by using the fact that the operator norm
\left\|\cdots\right\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\,\sim\,1
, we get

\left|I_{\beta,2}\right|\lesssim\left|\beta\right|^{-1}\,\left\|{\bf K}\left(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Knowing that the operator
{\bf K}
, when reduced to
\mathbb{H}_{0}(\mathrm{div}\,=0)
, behaves proportional to
k^{2}\,{\bf N}^{k}
, we reduce the previous estimation to

\left|I_{\beta,2}\right|\lesssim\left|\beta\right|^{-1}\,k^{2}\;\left\|{\bf N}^{k}\left(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\left|\beta\right|^{-1}\,k^{2}\,\left\|{\bf N}^{k}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\;\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

As
\left\|{\bf N}^{k}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega))}
is of order one, and by the use of
(\ref{Equa426})
, we get

\left|I_{\beta,2}\right|\lesssim\left|\beta\right|^{-1}\,k^{2}\,k\;\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

Hence, by recalling
(\ref{EqWillUS})
, we obtain

(3.56)

\left\langle H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,=\,\frac{i\;k}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{6}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

Now, by going back to
(\ref{E-new})
, using
(\ref{Equa0858})
and
(\ref{def-xi})
, and recalling the fact that
\xi\sim 1
, we get

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pm\,12\,k^{2}\,\xi}{\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{7}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

Moreover, as
k^{2}\lesssim\left|\beta\right|
, see
(\ref{kscalebeta})
, we reduce the error term of the previous term to
\mathcal{O}\left(\dfrac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right)
. Hence,

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,k^{2}\,\xi}{\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})}{=}

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,k^{2}\,\xi_{m_{0}}}{\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi_{m_{0}}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\pm 3k^{2}\pi^{6}\beta}{(\pi^{3}+4\xi)(\pi^{3}+4\xi_{m_{0}})}\hat{x}\times\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}dz\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

We estimate the second term on the R.H.S as

\left|\frac{\pm 3k^{2}\pi^{6}\beta}{(\pi^{3}+4\xi)(\pi^{3}+4\xi_{m_{0}})}\hat{x}\times\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}dz\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|=\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right).

Then,

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{\mathrm{eff},-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,k^{2}\,\xi_{m_{0}}}{\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi_{m_{0}}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{Expressionximo0})}{=}

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\;\frac{1}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{WBMX})}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\;\left|3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right|^{2}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

At this stage, by combining the previous formula with (
3.49
), we obtain

\displaystyle E^{\infty}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\;\left|3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right|^{2}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right)+{\mathcal{O}}\left(c(k,\eta_{0},c_{r},c_{0})\;g_{c_{r}}(a)\right).

Based on the fact that
[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}
is of order one with respect to
a
, the definition of
c(k,\eta_{0},c_{r},c_{0})
and the estimation of
g_{c_{r}}(a)
, given by
(\ref{add-constant})
, we deduce that

\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right)\,+\,{\mathcal{O}}\left(c(k,\eta_{0},c_{r},c_{0})\;[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\;g_{c_{r}}(a)\right)\;=\;\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

Hence, by taking into account that we have assumed that
\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,>\,\dfrac{1}{3}
, we end up with the coming formula

(3.57)

\displaystyle E^{\infty}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\;\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right)^{2}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

Recalling that
H^{Inc}(x):=\left(\theta\,\times\,\mathrm{p}\right)\,e^{i\,k\,\theta\cdot x}
, using the smallness of the frequency
k
and the boundedness of the operator
\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}
, to get

\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}H^{Inc},e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,=\,\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\left(\theta\,\times\,\mathrm{p}\right),e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k\right).

And, this allows us to rewrite
(\ref{E-new-2})
as

(3.58)

\displaystyle E^{\infty}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\;\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right)^{2}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\left\langle\left({\bf I}-\frac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\left(\theta\,\times\,\mathrm{p}\right),e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

By utilizing the Born series and keeping the dominant term associated with the operator
{\bf K}
, we can demonstrate that

\left({\bf I}\,-\,\dfrac{\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\,=\,\LARGE\textbf{N}^{-1}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(\beta\right),

where
N
is a self-adjoint operator given by

\LARGE\textbf{N}:=\left({\bf I}\,-\,\dfrac{\pm\,k^{2}}{2\,\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}\left(N+N^{\prime}\right)\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,\right)

with
N
is the Newtonian operator and
N^{\prime}
is the operator defined by
(\ref{DefNprimeTHM})
. Using this, we rewrite
(\ref{Eq129})
as

E^{\infty}(\hat{x})=\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\;\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right)^{2}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\left\langle\LARGE\textbf{N}^{-1}\left(\theta\,\times\,\mathrm{p}\right),e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

Now, by taking the adjoint operator of
\LARGE\textbf{N}^{-1}
, which is itself, and remembering that
\langle;\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
is a complex inner product, we get

(3.59)

E^{\infty}(\hat{x})=\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\;\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right)^{2}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\;\left(\theta\,\times\,\mathrm{p}\right)\cdot\int_{\Omega}\overline{\LARGE\textbf{N}^{-1}\left(e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right)}(x)\,dx+\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

We set
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{X}}_{m_{0}}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)
to be the tensor defined by

(3.60)

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{X}}_{m_{0}}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta):=\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\otimes\int_{\Omega}\overline{\LARGE\textbf{N}^{-1}\left(e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right)}(x)\,dx.

Finally, by the use of the definition
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{X}}_{m_{0}}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)
, the formula
(\ref{MNP})
can be rewritten like

(3.61)

E^{\infty}(\hat{x})=\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\;\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)-1\right)^{2}}\,\hat{x}\,\times\,\left(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{X}}_{m_{0}}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)\cdot\left(\theta\,\times\,\mathrm{p}\right)\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\right).

Therefore, for
k
fulfilling
(\ref{kfreqplasmon})
, we see that the first term in (
3.57
) is the dominant part and it behaves like
\dfrac{k^{2}}{\beta}

which indicates that with
\beta\,\ll\,1
and
\eta_{0}\,\gg\,1
, we deduce a giant amplification of the magnetic field
H^{Inc}
. In other words, the incident frequency

k_{n_{0}}^{2}\,:=\,\frac{1}{\eta_{0}\,\,\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(1)}(B)},

is a low frequency plasmonic resonance generated in
\Omega
by the cluster of the all-dielectric nano-particles in the quasi-static regime.

The previous proof supports the following two remarks.

Remark 3.4
.

(1)

The effective permeability expression. Recall, from
(\ref{new-coeff-neg})
, that

\mathring{\mu_{r}}\,=\,\frac{\pi^{3}\,-\,8\,\xi}{\pi^{3}\,+\,4\,\xi}\;{\bf I}\overset{(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})}{=}\frac{1\,-\,2\,\left[\dfrac{1}{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)}\,+\,\beta\right]}{1\,+\,\left[\dfrac{1}{\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)}\,+\,\beta\right]}\,{\bf I}=\left(1\,-\,\frac{1}{\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)}+\mathrm{T}\left(\beta\right)\right)\,{\bf I}

where

\mathrm{T}\left(\beta\right):=\frac{-\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,2\,+\,\dfrac{1}{3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\right)}{\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,+\,\beta\,\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,\dfrac{1}{3}\right)}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(\beta\right).

(2)

The scale of
\beta
relative to
k
. We have seen, from
(\ref{kscalebeta})
, that
\beta\sim k^{2}
. But, if
\beta
can be taken to satisfy
\beta\,\sim\,k^{\sigma}
, with
0\,<\sigma\,<2
, the following approximation holds

\left({\bf I}-\frac{\left(\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}\,{\bf K}\right)^{\star}}{f(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)}\right)^{-1}\left(e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\right)\;=\;e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\;+\;\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2-\sigma}\right).

And,
(\ref{DefTensorX})
can be approximated by

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{X}}_{m_{0}}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)\;=\;{\bf Q}^{(3)}_{m_{0}}\;+\;\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2-\sigma}\right).

where

(3.62)

{\bf Q}^{(3)}_{m_{0}}\,:=\,\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\otimes\int_{\Omega}\overline{e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}}(z)\,dz.

Consequently,

E^{\infty}(\hat{x})=\frac{\pm\,3\,k^{2}}{\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}\;\hat{x}\,\times\left({\bf Q}^{(3)}_{m_{0}}\cdot\left(\theta\,\times\,\mathrm{p}\right)\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{\min\left(4-2\,\sigma;2\;3-\sigma\right)}\right).

In the case of plasmonics, the tensor
{\bf Q}^{(3)}_{m_{0}}
plays the same role as the tensor
{\bf P}_{0}
, given in
(\ref{defP0})
.

This ends the proof of the fourth part.

Observe that by construction of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{X}}_{m_{0}}(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(\Omega),\beta)
, see for instance
(\ref{DefTensorX})
, if
\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz=0
, the far field formula given by
(\ref{AnLoIs})
will be meaningless (i.e. the dominant term vanishes). Unfortunately, depending on the
\Omega
’s shape, it may happen that
\int_{\Omega}e_{m_{0}}^{(3)}(z)\;dz
may be a vanishing integral. For example, based on
[
24
, Section 1.3]
, we know that for the case
\Omega
is the unit ball, we have
\int_{\Omega=B(0,1)}e_{n}^{(3)}(z)\;dz\,=\,0
, for
n\geq 2
. The coming lines comes to overcome this issue and brings more details on the case
\Omega
is the unit ball.

In the sequel, we assume that
\Omega
is the unit ball and we take the parameter
\xi
to be large enough, i.e.
\xi\gg 1
. Equations
(\ref{Eq455})
and
(\ref{add-coro-E})
illustrate that choosing the lower sign for
{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
is crucial for creating a resonance phenomenon related to eigenvalues
\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,>\,\dfrac{1}{3}
, see for instance
(\ref{ExcSeqxn})
. Our subsequent focus, guided by an essential detail that we will highlight later, is on the case where
\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)=\dfrac{1}{3}
and
{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
generated by the upper sign. We start by recalling, from
(\ref{Eq455})
, the far-field formula

E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})=-\,\pm\,i\,k\,\xi\,\frac{12}{(\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi)}\int_{\Omega}e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}\hat{x}\times H^{\mu}(z)\,dz,

which by Taylor expansion, for the function
z\,\rightarrow\,e^{-\,i\,k\,\hat{x}\cdot z}
, and the fact that we take
\xi\,\gg\,1
, we obtain

E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})=-\,\pm\,i\,k\,\xi\,\frac{12}{(\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi)}\int_{\Omega}\hat{x}\times H^{\mu}(z)\,dz+\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

and using the fact that

\int_{\Omega}e_{n}^{(1)}(x)\,dx\,=\int_{\Omega}e_{n+1}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\,=\,0,\quad\text{for}\quad n\geq 1,

we rewrite the previous expression of the far-field like

(3.63)

E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})=-\,\pm\,i\,k\,\xi\,\frac{12}{(\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi)}\int_{\Omega}\hat{x}\times e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\;\langle H^{\mu};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right).

Now, we need to estimate
\langle H^{\mu};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
. From the L.S.E given by
(\ref{LS-vec})
, we have

(3.64)

H^{\mu}\,+\,\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)}\,\nabla{\bf M}(H^{\mu})\,=\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}\,+\,\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)}\,K(H^{\mu}),

where
K(H^{\mu}):=-\left(\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\,-\,\nabla{\bf M}\right)(H^{\mu})\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}(H^{\mu})
. Moreover, from
(\ref{mid-diff})
, we know that

(3.65)

\displaystyle K(H^{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2}\,N(H^{\mu})\,+\,\frac{k^{2}}{2}N^{\prime}(H^{\mu})\,+\frac{i\,k^{3}}{6\,\pi}\int_{\Omega}H^{\mu}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\,\pi}\sum_{n\geq 3}\,\frac{(i\,k)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!}\,\int_{\Omega}\nabla\nabla\left|\cdot-y\right|^{n}\cdot H^{\mu}(y)\,dy+\frac{k^{2}}{4\,\pi}\sum_{n\geq 1}\,\frac{(i\,k)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!}\,\int_{\Omega}\left|\cdot-y\right|^{n}\,H^{\mu}(y)\,dy,

with
N^{\prime}(\cdot)
is the operator defined by
(\ref{DefNprimeTHM})
. Hence, from
(\ref{LSE13})
and the fact that
\lambda_{1}^{(3)}(\Omega)=\dfrac{1}{3}
, we get

\langle H^{\mu};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\;\frac{\pi^{3}}{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi\right)}\,=\,i\,k\,\langle H^{Inc};e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)}\,\langle K(H^{\mu});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Based on
(\ref{Proj2=0})
, we split
H^{\mu}\,=\,\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\,+\,\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})
and we plug it into the previous equation to obtain

\displaystyle\langle H^{\mu};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\;\frac{\pi^{3}}{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\,k\,\langle H^{Inc};e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)}\,\langle K(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu}));e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)}\,\sum_{j\geq 1}\,\langle H^{\mu};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

This implies,

(3.66)

\displaystyle\langle H^{\mu};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi\right)}{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}i\,k\,\langle H^{Inc};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\,\langle K(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu}));e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{12\,\xi}{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\,\sum_{j\geq 2}\,\langle H^{\mu};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

The dispersion equation related to this case will be given by

0\,=\,\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

which can be rewritten using
(\ref{ANC})
, as

(3.67)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{3}\,-\,6\,\xi\,k^{2}\,\Bigg[\langle N(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\langle N^{\prime}(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\frac{i\,k}{3\,\pi}\left|\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2\,\pi}\,\,\sum_{n\geq 3}\,\frac{(i\,k)^{n-1}}{(n+1)!}\,\langle\int_{\Omega}\nabla\nabla\left|\cdot-y\right|^{n}\cdot e_{1}^{(3)}(y)\,dy;e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2\,\pi}\sum_{n\geq 1}\,\frac{(i\,k)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!}\,\langle\int_{\Omega}\left|\cdot-y\right|^{n}\,e_{1}^{(3)}(y)\,dy;e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg].

Let us postpone the computations and mention first the following comment. In contrast to the case of eigenvalues
\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)>\dfrac{1}{3}
, where the choice of a lower sign for the tensor
{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
was important to generate resonances. For the case of
\lambda_{1}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,=\,\dfrac{1}{3}
, we made the choice to take the lower sign for the tensor
{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
. Since
k^{2}
is proportional to
\xi
, this choice of sign produces a dispersion equation that does not have a monomial of order one in
\xi
. Thus, the dominant part of the dispersion equation will take the following form

a_{0}\,+\,a_{1}\,\xi^{2}\,+\,a_{2}\,\xi^{\frac{5}{2}},\quad\text{with}\quad a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\,\in\mathbb{C}.

Then, by cancelling the quadratic part and taking into account the fact that
\xi\gg 1
, we will have an amplification of the incident field. More details will be given later.

Now, by going back to
(\ref{Eq0408})
, assuming that

\langle\left(N\,+\,N^{\prime}\right)(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\neq\,0,

the solution, that we denote by
\xi_{1}
, to the following reduced dispersion equation

0\,=\,\pi^{3}\,-\,6\,\xi\,k^{2}\,\langle\left(N\,+\,N^{\prime}\right)(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\overset{(\ref{def-xi})}{=}\,\pi^{3}\,-\,6\,\xi^{2}\,\frac{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}{\eta_{0}}\,\langle\left(N\,+\,N^{\prime}\right)(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

is given by

(3.68)

\xi_{1}^{2}\,=\,\frac{\pi^{3}\,\eta_{0}}{6\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,\langle\left(N\,+\,N^{\prime}\right)(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}.

For
\xi\gg 1
, the coefficient of the third term in
(\ref{IHIP})
is of order one with respect to the frequency
k
. To improve the estimation of
\langle H^{\mu};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
, in
(\ref{IHIP})
, we need to compute
\langle H^{\mu};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
, for
j\geq 2
. Again, by using the L.S.E given by
(\ref{LSE13})
, we get

(3.69)

\langle H^{\mu};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,=\,\frac{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,i\,k}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,+\,12\,\xi\,\lambda_{j}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\,\langle H^{Inc};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\,\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,+\,12\,\xi\,\lambda_{j}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(H^{\mu});e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Then, by plugging (
3.69
) into
(\ref{IHIP})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle H^{\mu};e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\Bigg[\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi\right)ik\langle H^{Inc};e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+12\xi\langle K(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu}));e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 12\,\xi\,\sum_{j\geq 2}\,\frac{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,i\,k}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,+\,12\,\xi\,\lambda_{j}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\,\langle H^{Inc};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\,\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 12\,\xi\,\sum_{j\geq 2}\,\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,+\,12\,\xi\,\lambda_{j}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(H^{\mu});e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg].

By using the previous formula and going back to
(\ref{FFEI})
, the far-field takes the following form

(3.70)

E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})=\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi\,k^{2}}{\left(\pi^{3}-12\,\xi\,\langle K\left(e_{1}^{3}\right);e_{1}^{3}\rangle\right)}\int_{\Omega}\hat{x}\times e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\;\langle H^{Inc};e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+Err_{0}+\mathcal{O}\left(k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

where

\displaystyle Err_{0}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mp\,\frac{i\,k\,144\,\xi^{2}}{(\pi^{3}-4\,\xi)\,\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\,\int_{\Omega}\hat{x}\times e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\,\Bigg[\langle K(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu}));e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 2}\,\frac{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,i\,k}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,+\,12\,\xi\,\lambda_{j}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\,\langle H^{Inc};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j\geq 2}\,\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)\,+\,12\,\xi\,\lambda_{j}^{(3)}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(H^{\mu});e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg].

Now, by taking the modulus on the both sides of the previous formula, we get

\displaystyle\left|Err_{0}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{k\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\,\Bigg[\left|\langle K(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu}));e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 2}k\,\left|\langle H^{Inc};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\,\left|\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|+\,\sum_{j\geq 2}\left|\langle K(H^{\mu});e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\,\left|\langle K(e_{j}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{k\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\,\Bigg[\left\|K(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu}))\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left(k\,+\,\left\|K(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\,\left\|K(e_{1}^{(3)})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg].

And, by keeping the dominant part of the operator
K(\cdot)
, which is
\dfrac{k^{2}}{2}\,\left(N\,+\,N^{\prime}\right)
, see for instance
(\ref{ANC})
, and using the fact that
N^{\prime}(\cdot)
behaves like
N(\cdot)
from singularity analysis point of view we get from the previous formula

\left|Err_{0}\right|\lesssim\frac{k\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\,\left[k^{2}\,\left\|N(\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu}))\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\,\left(k^{3}\,+k^{4}\,\left\|N(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)\,\left\|N(e_{1}^{(3)})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right].

Moreover, using the continuity of the Newtonian operator, we obtain

\left|Err_{0}\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{3}\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\,\left[\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+k+k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right].

As
\left\|H^{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
and using the smallness of the frequency
k
, we obtain from the previous equation

(3.71)

\left|Err_{0}\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{3}\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\,\left[\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+k+k^{2}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right].

To estimate the R.H.S of the above equation, we need to estimate
\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
and
\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
.

(1)

Estimation of
\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
. To achieve this, we go back to the L.S.E, given by
(\ref{LSE13})
, to get

\langle H^{\mu};e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\,i\,k\,\langle H^{Inc};e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\frac{12\,\xi}{(\pi^{3}-4\xi)}\,\langle K(H^{\mu});e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

which, after taking the modulus, the dominant part of the operator
K(\cdot)
and the fact that
\xi\gg 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\langle H^{\mu};e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k\,\left|\langle H^{Inc};e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\,+k^{2}\,\left|\langle N(H^{\mu});e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|

\displaystyle\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{Inc})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{Inc})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

(2)

Estimation of
\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
. To achieve this, we go back to
(\ref{West})
, to get

\displaystyle\left|\langle H^{\mu};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k\,\left|\langle H^{Inc};e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\,+k^{2}\,\left|\langle N(H^{\mu});e_{j}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|

\displaystyle\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{Inc})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mu}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k\,\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{Inc})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

This gives us

\left\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\mathcal{O}\left(k\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(H^{\mu})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\mathcal{O}\left(k\right).

And, by going back to
(\ref{USUK})
, we obtain

\left|Err_{0}\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{4}\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|},

which implies, by recalling
(\ref{EqSN})
,

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi\,k^{2}}{\left(\pi^{3}-12\,\xi\,\langle K\left(e_{1}^{3}\right);e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\hat{x}\times\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\;\langle H^{Inc};e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi\,k^{2}}{\left(\pi^{3}-12\,\xi\,\langle K\left(e_{1}^{3}\right);e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\hat{x}\times\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\;\left(\theta\times\mathrm{p}\right)\cdot\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\,

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi\,k^{2}}{\left(\pi^{3}-12\,\xi\,\langle K\left(e_{1}^{3}\right);e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\hat{x}\times\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\;\int_{\Omega}\left(\theta\times\mathrm{p}\right)\cdot\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{(i\,k\,\theta\cdot z)^{n}}{n!}\cdot e_{1}^{3}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{4}\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{3}\right).

The second term on the R.H.S can be estimated as

\left|\cdots\right|\,\lesssim\,\frac{\xi\,k^{3}}{\left|\pi^{3}-12\,\xi\,\langle K\left(e_{1}^{3}\right);e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}

Hence,

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=
