\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi\,k^{2}}{\left(\pi^{3}-12\,\xi\,\langle K\left(e_{1}^{3}\right);e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\hat{x}\times\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\;\left(\theta\times\mathrm{p}\right)\cdot\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(z)\,dz\,

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{3}\right),

which can be rewritten like

(3.72)

\displaystyle E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\,\frac{12\,\xi\,k^{2}}{\left(\pi^{3}-12\,\xi\,\langle K\left(e_{1}^{3}\right);e_{1}^{3}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right)}\hat{x}\times\left(\textbf{Q}_{1}\cdot\left(\theta\times\mathrm{p}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{k^{3}\,\,\xi}{\left|\pi^{3}\,-\,12\,\xi\,\langle K(e_{1}^{(3)});e_{1}^{(3)}\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right|}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(k^{3}\right),

where the tensor
\textbf{Q}_{1}
is given by
(\ref{DefQm0Q1})
.
In particular, when
\xi=\xi_{1}
, with
\xi_{1}
is given by
(\ref{SolRDEq})
, the dominant term in
(\ref{Eq1614})
behaves like
\dfrac{1}{k}
, we recall that the frequency
k
is taken to be small, and the error term will be of order one. More precisely, we get

E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})=\frac{\pm\,i\,6\,\pi}{k\,\left|\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right|^{2}}\,\hat{x}\times\left(\textbf{Q}_{1}\cdot\left(\theta\times\mathrm{p}\right)\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(1\right).

In addition, as we are dealing with a ball and thanks to
[
23
, Section 4.5.3]
, we know that
7
7

7

In this particular case of the unit ball, we have

\int_{\Omega=B(0,1)}e_{1}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\;=\;\frac{\pi^{2}}{9}\,\int_{B(0,1)}\phi_{1}(x)\,dx,

where the R.H.S is given in
(\ref{PCB})
.

\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\\
-\,i\,\dfrac{2\,\sqrt{6\,\pi}}{9}\\
\\
\dfrac{2}{3}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{\pi}{3}}\end{pmatrix}.

This implies

\left|\int_{\Omega}e_{1}^{(3)}(x)\,dx\right|\;=\;\frac{2\,\sqrt{\pi}}{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\textbf{Q}_{1}\;=\;\frac{4\,\pi}{27}\,\textbf{I}.

Hence,

E^{\infty}_{eff,-}(\hat{x})\,=\,\pm\,i\,2\,\pi\,k^{-1}\,\hat{x}\times\left(\theta\times\mathrm{p}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(1\right),

which, combined with
(\ref{add-coro-4})
, gives us the coming formula

E^{\infty}(\hat{x})\,=\,\pm\,i\,2\,\pi\,k^{-1}\,\hat{x}\times\left(\theta\times\mathrm{p}\right)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\left(1\right).

This concludes the proof of the fifth part and ends the proof of
Theorem
1.6
.

4.
A-priori estimates for the point-interaction approximations

In this section, we prove the a-priori estimates presented in
Proposition
2.3
.
We recall the L.S.E introduced in (
1.37
). By taking the average value with respect to
x
over
S_{m}
, there holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\left[-\,\underset{x}{\nabla}{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right](x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{Inc}(x)\,dx.

where
\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega}
is the Magnetization operator defined in
\Omega
,
{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega}
is the Newtonian operator defined in
\Omega
and
S_{m}
is the largest ball contained in
\Omega_{m}
. As
\Omega\,=\,\left(\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}\right)\cup\left(\Omega\setminus\left(\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}\right)\right),
we rewrite the previous equation as

(4.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\sum_{j=1}^{\aleph}\left[-\,\underset{x}{\nabla}{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{j}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{j}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right](x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{Inc}(x)\,dx\,+\,C_{m},

where

\displaystyle C_{m}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\left[-\,\underset{x}{\nabla}{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega\setminus\left(\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}\right)}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega\setminus\left(\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}\right)}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right](x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\,\left[-\,\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{S_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{S_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right](x)\,dx.

Using Taylor expansion for
H^{Inc}(\cdot)
and splitting
\underset{j=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{j}=\underset{j=1\atop j\neq m}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{j}\,\cup\,\Omega_{m}
, we rewrite
(\ref{EqLK1})
as

(4.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(x)\,dx\,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\left[-\,\underset{x}{\nabla}{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{j}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{j}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right](x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}(z_{m})\,+\,T_{m}\,+\,B_{m}\,+\,C_{m},

where

\displaystyle T_{m}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\frac{ik}{\left|S_{m}\right|}\int_{S_{m}}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla H^{Inc}(z_{m}+t(x-z_{m}))\cdot(x-z_{m})\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle B_{m}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\left[-\,\underset{x}{\nabla}{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right](x)\,dx.

We add and subtract its discrete version to obtain the second term on the L.H.S of
(\ref{EqLK2})
. More precisely, we get

(4.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\Upsilon_{k}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}(z_{m})+A_{m}+T_{m}+B_{m}+C_{m},

where

\displaystyle A_{m}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\left[-\,\underset{x}{\nabla}{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{j}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{j}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\right](x)\,dx

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\Upsilon_{k}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy.

For shortness, we set the following notation

(4.4)

Error_{m}^{\star}:=T_{m}+A_{m}+B_{m}+C_{m},

and we rewrite
(\ref{eq-ls-aver0})
as

(4.5)

\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(x)\,dx\,-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\Upsilon_{k}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\,=\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}(z_{m})\,+\,Error_{m}^{\star}.

Our aim is to evaluate
Error_{m}^{\star}
by estimating
T_{m}
,
A_{m}
,
B_{m}
and
C_{m}
.

(1)

Estimation of
T_{m}
. We have,

\displaystyle T_{m}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{ik}{\left|S_{m}\right|}\int_{S_{m}}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla H^{Inc}(z_{m}+t(x-z_{m}))\cdot(x-z_{m})\,dt\,dx

\displaystyle\left|T_{m}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{\left|S_{m}\right|}\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla H^{Inc}(z_{m}+t(\cdot-z_{m}))\,dt\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(S_{m})}\,\left\|(\cdot-z_{m})\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(S_{m})}=\mathcal{O}\left(k\,d\right).

Hence, using the fact
\aleph=\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-3}\right)
,

(4.6)

\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\sum}}\left|T_{m}\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\,\aleph\,d^{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-1}\right).

(2)

Estimation of
A_{m}
. We start by rewriting
A_{m}
as

(4.7)

A_{m}=\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\;\left[A_{m,1}+A_{m,2}+A_{m,3}+A_{m,4}\right],

and then we set and we estimate each term appearing on the R.H.S of (
4.7
), separately.

(a)

Estimation of
A_{m,1}
.

A_{m,1}:=-\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\,\int_{\Omega_{j}}\,\nabla{\bf M}_{S_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(\cdot)\,-\,\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)\,\right)(z)\,dz,

Then, by taking the modulus on the both sides of the previous equation, based on
Corollary
2.2
, we get

\left|A_{m,1}\right|\lesssim\,\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|\;[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\;\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\int_{S_{m}}\frac{1}{\left|x-z\right|^{3}}\,dx\,dz.

Indeed, with the Taylor expansion near the centers
(z_{m},z_{j})
for the function over the double integral, by using the counting lemma given by
Lemma
6.1
, since
|\Omega_{j}|\sim d^{3}
, we obtain that

\left|A_{m,1}\right|\lesssim\,\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|\;[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\;\left|S_{m}\right|\,\left|\log(d)\right|.

Hence, using the fact that
\aleph=\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-3}\right)
,

(4.8)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\;\left|A_{m,1}\right|^{2}\lesssim\,\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|^{2}\;[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{2\,\alpha-3}\;\left|\log(d)\right|^{2}.

(b)

Estimation of
A_{m,2}
.

A_{m,2}\,:=\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\,\int_{\Omega_{j}}\,\left[-\,\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{S_{m}}+k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{S_{m}}+\nabla{\bf M}_{S_{m}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(\cdot)\,-\,\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)\right)\,dx.

Regarding the singularity analysis of the kernel of the operator
\left[-\,\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{S_{m}}+k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{S_{m}}+\nabla{\bf M}_{S_{m}}\right]
, for any
x\in S_{m}\subset\Omega_{m}
and
z\in\Omega_{j}
,
j\neq m
, we have

(4.9)

\left|\left(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right)(x,z)\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{d_{mj}},

see
[
12
, Inequality (2.4.22)]
. Then, by using
(\ref{distribute})
,
(\ref{Upsk-Ups0})
,
Corollary
2.2
and
Lemma
6.1
, we obtain

\left|A_{m,2}\right|\lesssim k^{2}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\;[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}d^{\alpha}\,\left|S_{m}\right|.

Hence,

(4.10)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\,\left|A_{m,2}\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\,k^{4}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\;[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\;d^{2\,\alpha-3}.

(c)

Estimation of
A_{m,3}
.

A_{m,3}:=\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\left(\int_{\Omega_{j}}\int_{S_{m}}\Upsilon_{0}(x,z)\,dx\,dz\,-d^{3}\,\left|S_{m}\right|\,\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy.

Since
\Upsilon_{0}(x,z)
, for
x
and
z
in two disjoint domains, is an harmonic function, we get by the Mean Value Theorem,

\int_{S_{m}}\Upsilon_{0}(x,z)\,dx=\left|S_{m}\right|\,\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z),\quad z\in\Omega_{j}.

This implies,

A_{m,3}=\left|S_{m}\right|\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\left(\int_{\Omega_{j}}\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z)\,dz\,-\,d^{3}\,\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy.

Now, for any fixed
m
, with
m\in\left\{1,\cdots,\aleph\right\}
, knowing that

\left|\Omega_{j}\right|\sim d^{3}
and the fact that
|S_{m}|=|S_{j}|
, for any
m,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,\aleph\right\}
, we can further derive that

A_{m,3}=\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\left(\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z)-\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\,dz\,\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy,

Now, for any fixed
m
, with
m=1,\cdots,\aleph
, we split
\Omega
into two parts as

\Omega=\Omega_{0}^{m}\cup\left(\Omega\backslash\Omega_{0}^{m}\right),

where
\Omega_{0}^{m}
is a small neighborhood of
\Omega_{m}
such that there exists
\beta\in(0,1)
fulfilling

(4.11)

|\Omega_{0}^{m}|={\mathcal{O}}\left(d^{3\beta}\right),\quad\mathrm{diam}(\Omega_{0}^{m})={\mathcal{O}}(d^{\beta}),

see Figure
2
for a schematic illustration.

Figure 2.
A schematic illustration for the way of splitting
\Omega
in terms of
\Omega_{m}
.

Denote the number of those
\Omega_{j}^{\prime}s
with
\Omega_{j}\cap\Omega_{0}^{m}\neq\{\emptyset\}
by
N_{(1)}
and the corresponding subdomains as
\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}
,
j=1,\cdots,N_{(1)}
. We set the number of the rest of
\Omega_{j}^{\prime}s
as
{N}_{(2)}
and we denote those subdomains as
\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{out}}
, then we have

(4.12)

\Omega_{0}^{m}=\underset{j=1}{\overset{N_{(1)}}{\cup}}\left(\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\cap\Omega_{0}^{m}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Omega\backslash\Omega_{0}^{m}=\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{{N}_{(2)}}{\cup}}\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{out}}\right)\cup\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{N_{(1)}}{\cup}}\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\backslash\Omega_{0}^{m}\right).

Thus, there holds

(4.13)

A_{m,3}=A_{m,3,1}+A_{m,3,2}+A_{m,3,3},

where

\displaystyle A_{m,3,1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(2)}}\int_{\Omega_{j}^{out}}\left(\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z)-\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\,dz\,\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle A_{m,3,2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{N_{(1)}}\int_{\Omega_{j}^{int}\setminus\Omega_{0}^{m}}\left(\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z)-\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\,dz\,\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle A_{m,3,3}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{N_{(1)}}\int_{\Omega_{j}^{int}\cap\Omega_{0}^{m}}\left(\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z)-\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\,dz\,\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy.

Since

\underset{j=1}{\overset{N_{(1)}}{\cup}}\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\backslash\Omega_{0}^{m}\subset\Omega\backslash\Omega_{0}^{m}\quad\mbox{and}\quad N_{(1)}\ll{N}_{(2)}\mbox{ with }{N}_{(2)}\sim d^{-3},

the estimation of
A_{m,3,2}
is dominated by the estimation of
A_{m,3,1}
. Then we have

\left|A_{m,3,1}+A_{m,3,2}\right|\;\lesssim\;\left|A_{m,3,1}\right|.

To estimate
A_{m,3,1}
, we use Taylor expansion near
z_{j}
to obtain

A_{m,3,1}=\,\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(2)}}\int_{\Omega_{j}^{out}}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j}+t(z-z_{j}))\cdot(z-z_{j})\,dt\,dz\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy,

which can be estimated as

\left|A_{m,3,1}\right|\lesssim\,\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(2)}}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{4}}\,\int_{\Omega_{j}^{out}}\left|z-z_{j}\right|\,dz\;\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|\;\left|S_{j}\right|\;\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(S_{j})}.

Since
\left|\Omega_{j}^{out}\right|\sim d^{3}
, for
1\leq j\leq N_{(2)}
, we can further obtain that

\left|A_{m,3,1}\right|\lesssim\left|S_{m}\right|\,d^{4}\;\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|\;\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(2)}}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{4}}.

From
Lemma
6.1
, we know that

\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(2)}}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{4}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\left(\underset{m\neq j}{\min}\;\;d_{mj}\right)^{4}},

where
d_{mj}\geq\underset{m\neq j}{\min}\;\;d_{mj}\gtrsim d^{\beta}
for
z_{m}\in\Omega_{m}
and
z_{j}\in\Omega_{j}^{out}
,
1\leq j\leq N_{(2)}
, which implies,

\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(2)}}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{4}}\lesssim\,d^{-4\,\beta}.

Then,

\left|A_{m,3,1}\right|\lesssim\left|S_{m}\right|\,d^{4-4\,\beta}\;\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|\;\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}.

and thus

(4.14)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\;\left|A_{m,3,1}\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\,d^{5-8\,\beta}\;\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|^{2}\;\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)},

due to the relation that
\aleph\sim d^{-3}
.

For
A_{m,3,3}
, by taking the Taylor expansion of the function
\left(\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z)-\Upsilon_{0}(z_{m},z_{j})\right)
at
z=z_{j}
and using the fact that
\left|\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\cap\Omega_{0}^{m}\right|\sim d^{3}
, for
1\leq j\leq N_{(1)}
,we can know that

\left|A_{m,3,3}\right|\,\lesssim\,\left|S_{m}\right|\;\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\;d^{4}\;\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{N_{(1)}}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{4}},

and
8
8

8

We have

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{N_{(1)}}\,\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{8}}\,=\,\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(1)}}\,\sum_{m=1\atop m\neq j}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{8}}.

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\frac{\left|A_{m,3,3}\right|^{2}}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\;d^{8}\;N_{(1)}\;\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{N_{(1)}}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{8}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\;d^{8}\;N_{(1)}\;\,\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(1)}}\,\sum_{m=1\atop m\neq j}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{8}}\,\lesssim\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\;N_{(1)}^{2},

by utilizing
Lemma

6.1
.
For
N_{(1)}
, since
\Omega_{m}\subset\Omega_{0}^{m}
and for any
\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}
, with
1\leq j\leq N_{(1)}
, we have
\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\cap\Omega_{0}^{m}\neq\{\emptyset\}
, then there exists a positive constant
\delta_{0}
such that

\left|\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\cap\Omega_{0}^{m}\right|\geq\delta_{0}\left|\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\right|>0,

which gives us

d^{3\beta}\overset{(\ref{Omega-0m})}{=}|\Omega_{0}^{m}|\overset{(\ref{OmegamOmegaint})}{=}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{(1)}}\left|\Omega_{j}^{\mathrm{int}}\cap\Omega_{0}^{m}\right|\geq N_{(1)}\delta_{0}d^{3},

indicating that

N_{(1)}\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-1}d^{3\beta-3}.

Then,

(4.15)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\;\left|A_{m,3,3}\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\;\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,\delta_{0}^{-2}\;d^{6\,\beta\,-\,6}.

Now, by recalling the expression
A_{m,3}
, given by
(\ref{Am3=Am31+Am32+Am33})
, using the fact that
A_{m,3,2}
is dominated by
A_{m,3,1}
, together with
(\ref{EstAm31})
and
(\ref{EstAm33})
, and by taking the parameter
9
9

9

The parameter
\beta
is chosen to equal the exponents of the parameter
d
in the estimations
(\ref{EstAm31})
and
(\ref{EstAm33})
.

\beta=\frac{11}{14}
, we obtain

(4.16)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\;\left|A_{m,3}\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\;\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\;d^{-\frac{9}{7}}.

(d)

Estimation of
A_{m,4}
.

A_{m,4}:=\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\left(\int_{\Omega_{j}}\int_{S_{m}}\left(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right)(x,z)\,dx\,dz\,-\,d^{3}\,\left|S_{m}\right|\,\left(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right)(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\cdot\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy.

Knowing that
\left|\Omega_{j}\right|\sim d^{3},
we rewrite
A_{m,4}
as

(4.17)

A_{m,4}=c_{r}^{-3}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\left(\int_{\Omega_{j}}\int_{S_{m}}\left(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right)(x,z)\,dx\,dz-\left|\Omega_{j}\right|\,\left|S_{m}\right|\,\left(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right)(z_{m},z_{j})\right)\cdot{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy.

By Taylor expansion of the function
\left(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right)(x,z)
at
(z_{m},z_{j})
, we have

(4.18)

(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0})(x,z)=(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0})(z_{m},z_{j})+R_{m,j}(x,z),

where the matrix
R_{m,j}(x,z)
is given by

\displaystyle R_{m,j}(x,z)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0})(z_{m}+t(x-z_{m}),z_{j})\cdot(x-z_{m})\,dt

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\underset{z}{\nabla}(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0})(z_{m},z_{j}+t(z-z_{j}))\cdot(z-z_{j})\,dt,

with

(4.19)

\left|R_{m,j}(x,z)\right|\overset{(\ref{Upsk-Ups0})}{\lesssim}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{2}}\,\left[\left|x-z_{m}\right|+\left|z-z_{j}\right|\right].

Then, by plugging
(\ref{CMPTaylor})
into
(\ref{Am4Formula})
, we can obtain

\displaystyle A_{m,4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\int_{S_{m}}R_{m,j}(x,z)\,dx\,dz\cdot\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{j}|}\int_{S_{j}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\left|A_{m,4}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{|Rmj|})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right|\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}\left(\Omega\right)}\,\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{d_{mj}^{2}}\,\left(\left|S_{m}\right|\,\int_{\Omega_{j}}\left|z-z_{j}\right|\,dz+\left|\Omega_{j}\right|\int_{S_{m}}\left|x-z_{m}\right|\,dx\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,d\,\left|S_{m}\right|\,\left\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rvert\;\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}\left(\Omega\right)},

by
Lemma

6.1
.
Hence,

(4.20)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\,\left|A_{m,4}\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\,d^{-1}\,\left\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rvert^{2}\;\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}\left(\Omega\right)}.

Therefore, for
A_{m}
, combining with the estimations
(\ref{EstimationAm1}),(\ref{EstimationAm2}),(\ref{EstAm3})
and
(\ref{EstimationAm4})
, we deduce that

(4.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|A_{m}\right|^{2}\;

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\;\sum_{j=1}^{4}\;\frac{1}{\left|S_{m}\right|^{2}}\;\left|A_{m,j}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}^{2}\,c_{r}^{6}}\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\;\left([H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{2\alpha-3}\,\left|\log d\right|^{2}\,+\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\,d^{-\frac{9}{7}}\right).

(3)

Estimation of
B_{m}
. We start by rewriting
B_{m}
as
B_{m}=B_{m,1}+B_{m,2}
, where

\displaystyle B_{m,1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm c_{0}}\frac{c_{r}^{-3}}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(\cdot)-\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)\right)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle B_{m,2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm c_{0}}\frac{c_{r}^{-3}}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)(x)\,dx.

Next, we estimate
B_{m,1}
and
B_{m,2}
, separately.

(a)

Estimation of
B_{m,1}
. Since
B_{m,1}
can be written as
10
10

10

We have used the fact that, for an arbitrary vector field
F
,

\int_{\Omega_{m}}\int_{S_{m}}\Upsilon_{0}(x,z)\cdot F(z)\,dx\,dz=\int_{S_{m}}\int_{\Omega_{m}}\Upsilon_{0}(x,z)\cdot F(z)\,dz\,dx=\int_{S_{m}}\nabla\nabla{\bf N}_{\Omega_{m}}\left(F\right)(x)\,dx.

(4.22)

\displaystyle B_{m,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm c_{0}}\frac{c_{r}^{-3}}{|S_{m}|}\Bigg[\int_{S_{m}}\nabla\nabla{\bf N}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(\cdot)-\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)\right)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{S_{m}}\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}+\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}+k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(\cdot)-\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)dy\right)\right)(x)dx\Bigg].

And, by taking the modulus on the both sides of (
4.22
), we get

\displaystyle\left|B_{m,1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}}\frac{c_{r}^{-3}}{|S_{m}|}\,|S_{m}|^{\frac{1}{q}}\,\left\|\nabla\nabla{\bf N}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(\cdot)-\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)\chi_{\Omega_{m}}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}(S_{m})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\,\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega_{m}}\int_{S_{m}}\left|\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right|(x,z)\,dx\,dz,

where
p,q>1
such that
\dfrac{1}{p}\,+\,\dfrac{1}{q}\,=\,1
. By utilizing the Calderon-Zygmund inequality, see
[
25
, Theorem 9.9]
, we can further get

\displaystyle\left|B_{m,1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}}\frac{c_{r}^{-3}}{|S_{m}|}\,|S_{m}|^{\frac{1}{q}}\,\left\|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(\cdot)-\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)\chi_{\Omega_{m}}(\cdot)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}(S_{m})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\,\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega_{m}}\int_{S_{m}}\left|\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right|(x,z)\,dx\,dz,

which can be reduced, with
Corollary
2.2
, to

\left|B_{m,1}\right|\lesssim\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\left\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rvert[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\,\left[1+\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{\Omega_{m}}\int_{S_{m}}\left|\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right|(x,z)\,dx\,dz\right].

Now, similar to
(\ref{Upsk-Ups0})
, for any
x\in S_{m}\subset\Omega_{m}
and
z\in\Omega_{m}
, we have

(4.23)

|(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0})(x,z)|\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{\left|x-z\right|},

hence,

\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega_{m}}\int_{S_{m}}\left|\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0}\right|(x,z)\,dx\,dz\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{\Omega_{m}}\int_{\Omega_{m}}\frac{1}{\left|x-z\right|}\,dx\,dz\lesssim k^{2}\,d^{2}.

Then,

(4.24)

\left|B_{m,1}\right|\lesssim\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}\,\left\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\rvert[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\,\left[1+k^{2}\,d^{2}\right]=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\right).

(b)

Estimation of
B_{m,2}
. We have,

\displaystyle B_{m,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\,\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\,\right)(x)\,dx}_{B_{m,2,1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\left[-\,\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{\Omega_{m}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)(x)\,dx}_{B_{m,2,2}}.

For
B_{m,2,2}
, similar to
(\ref{ImBBm1})
, there holds

(4.25)

\displaystyle\left|B_{m,2,2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}k^{4}}{c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega_{m}}\int_{S_{m}}\frac{1}{|x-z|}\,dx\,dz

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}k^{4}}{c_{0}}\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}c_{r}^{-3}\,d^{2}.

For
B_{m,2,1}
, as the function

x\longrightarrow\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\,\right)(x),

is harmonic one, by using the Mean Value Theorem, we get

\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\,\int_{S_{m}}\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\,\right)(x)\,dx\,=\,\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\,\right)(z_{m}),

and we end up with

\displaystyle B_{m,2,1}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right)(z_{m})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\left(\boldsymbol{I}\right)(z_{m})\cdot\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy\right),

where due to the constant vector nature of
{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy
, the second equality is result of it. By referring to
[
36
, Table 1]
, we know that

\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega_{m}}\left(\boldsymbol{I}\right)(x)\,=\,\frac{1}{3}\,\boldsymbol{I},\quad\text{for}\quad x\in\Omega_{m}.

Hence,

(4.26)

B_{m,2,1}=-\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm 3c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy.

Thus, for
B_{m}
, by using the estimates (
4.24
), (
4.25
) and the formula (
4.26
), we have

\displaystyle B_{m}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm 3c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy+B_{m,1}+B_{m,2,2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm 3c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\,[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{\alpha}\right),

where the remainder term admits the following estimation

(4.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|\mathcal{O}\left(\cdots\right)\right|^{2}\,\lesssim\,\mathcal{O}\left(\aleph\,\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}^{2}}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\,[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,c_{r}^{-6}\,d^{2\alpha}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}^{2}}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\,[H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\Omega)}\,c_{r}^{-6}\,d^{2\alpha-3}\right).

(4)

Estimation of
C_{m}
. We write
C_{m}
as

\displaystyle C_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\nabla{\bf M}_{S_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(x)\,dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{S_{m}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}_{S_{m}}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{S_{m}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(x)\,dx,

which, by utilizing the Mean Value Theorem, can be further formulated as

(4.28)

\displaystyle C_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\nabla{\bf M}_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(z_{m})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\left[-\nabla{\bf M^{k}}_{S_{m}}\,+\,\nabla{\bf M}_{S_{m}}\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}_{S_{m}}\right]\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)(x)\,dx.

Since for any
x\in S_{m}\subset\Omega_{m}
,
z\in\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}
, similar to (
4.23
), there holds

\left|(\Upsilon_{k}-\Upsilon_{0})(x,z)\right|\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{\left|x-z\right|},

then, by taking the modulus on the both sides of
(\ref{DefCm})
, we obtain

(4.29)

\displaystyle\left|C_{m}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\,\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\frac{1}{\left|z_{m}-z\right|^{3}}\;dz\;\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\int_{S_{m}}\frac{k^{2}}{\left|x-z\right|}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\;\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,dx\,dz}_{C_{m,2}}.

To estimate
C_{m,2}
, we consider the following two cases in terms of the split of
\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}
, for any fixed
m
.

(a)

We denote the set
V_{1}\subset\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}
, such that any
z\in V_{1}
locates away from
x\in S_{m}
. In this case, the function
\dfrac{1}{|x-z|}
is bounded. Moreover, from
Remark
1.1
, we know that
|V_{1}|={\mathcal{O}}(d)
.

(b)

We denote the set
V_{2}\subset\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}
such that
x\in S_{m}
locates near one of the
\Omega_{m}^{\prime}s
touching the boundary
\partial\Omega
of
V_{2}
. In this case, it is obvious that there exist a positive constant
c_{+}
such that
V_{2}\subset B(x,c_{+}d)
.

The figure below gives us a schematic illustration of the decomposition

(4.30)

\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}=V_{1}\cup V_{2}.

Figure 3.
A schematic illustration for the split of the region
\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}
.

Using the decomposition
(\ref{V1V2})
, we get

\displaystyle\left|C_{m,2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{V_{1}\cup V_{2}}\int_{S_{m}}\frac{1}{|x-z|}\,dx\,dz\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\left(|V_{1}|\,|S_{m}|+\int_{B(x,c_{+}d)}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\frac{1}{|x-z|}\,dx\,dz\,\right)\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)},

where

\int_{B(x,c_{+}d)}\,\int_{S_{m}}\,\frac{1}{|x-z|}\,dx\,dz=\mathcal{O}\left(\left|S_{m}\right|\;d^{2}\right),

together with the fact that
\left|V_{1}\right|=\mathcal{O}\left(d\right)
, which gives us

\left|C_{m,2}\right|\lesssim\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\;\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\;\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,d.

Now, the estimation for
C_{m}
in (
4.29
) becomes

(4.31)

\left|C_{m}\right|\lesssim\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\left[\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\frac{1}{\left|z_{m}-z\right|^{3}}\;dz+k^{2}\,d\right]\;\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right|\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}.

It is obvious that the first term on the R.H.S. of (
4.31
) is more dominant than the second one. Then, we can derive from (
4.31
) that

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|C_{m}\right|^{2}\lesssim\;\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}^{2}\,c_{r}^{6}}\,\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right|^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|\int_{\Omega\backslash\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\frac{1}{\left|z_{m}-z\right|^{3}}\;dz\right|^{2}.

By utilizing
Lemma
6.2
, formula
(\ref{count-bound})
, which provides a counting criterion near
\partial\Omega
, we obtain

(4.32)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|C_{m}\right|^{2}\;\lesssim\;\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}^{2}\,c_{r}^{6}}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\;\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\;d^{-1}.

Now, recall
Error_{m}^{\star}
given by (
4.4
), which can be written as

Error_{m}^{\star}=-\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm 3c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(y)\,dy+Error_{m},

with

(4.33)

Error_{m}:=T_{m}+A_{m}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,c_{r}^{-3}\,d^{2}\right)+C_{m}.

Using the expression of
Error_{m}
, given by (
4.33
), the L.S.E introduced in
(\ref{eq-ls-aver})
takes the following form

(4.34)

\digamma_{m}\,-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm c_{0}}a^{3-h}\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\Upsilon_{k}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot{\bf P}_{0}\cdot\digamma_{j}=\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}(z_{m})+Error_{m},

where

\digamma_{m}:=\left(\boldsymbol{I}+\frac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm 3c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{|S_{m}|}\int_{S_{m}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}(x)\,dx.

Taking into account the estimations
(\ref{EstTm})
,
(\ref{Am})
,
(\ref{ErrBm})
and
(\ref{Cm0})
, it is straightforward to derive the estimate associated with
Error_{m}
presented in (
4.33
) as

(4.35)

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left|Error_{m}\right|^{2}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\,\left[\left|T_{m}\right|^{2}\,+\left|A_{m}\right|^{2}\,+\left|\mathcal{O}(\cdots)\right|^{2}\,+\left|C_{m}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}\,k^{4}}{c_{0}^{2}}\,\lvert{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert^{2}\;c_{r}^{-6}\;\left([H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\,d^{2\alpha-3}\,\left|\log\left(d\right)\right|^{2}\,+\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rVert_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}\left(\Omega\right)}^{2}\,d^{-\frac{9}{7}}\right).

In addition, by subtracting (
1.33
) from (
4.34
), we get

(4.36)

\left(\digamma_{m}\,-\,U_{m}\,\right)\,-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm c_{0}}a^{3-h}\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}\Upsilon_{k}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot{\bf P_{0}}\cdot\left(\digamma_{j}\,-\,U_{j}\right)={Error}_{m},

where
{Error}_{m}
is given by
(\ref{DefErrorm})
. We know, from
(\ref{contrast-epsilon})
, that
\eta_{0}=\eta\,a^{2}
and by plugging this relation into
(\ref{diff-la})
, we derive the same algebraic system as the one given by
(\ref{linear-discrete})
, up to a switching operation
11
11

11

From a singularity analysis point of view, we have

\Upsilon_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)\cdot{\bf P_{0}}\;\sim\;{\bf P_{0}}\cdot\Upsilon_{k}(\cdot,\cdot).

between the matrix
\Upsilon_{k}(\cdot,\cdot)
and the tensor
{\bf P_{0}}
. To avoid redundancy steps, based on the proof of
[
15
, Theorem 1.3]
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\,\left|\digamma_{m}\,-\,U_{m}\right|^{2}\,\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\;

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;\frac{c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}{c_{r}^{3}\,c_{0}\,-k^{2}\,\eta_{0}\,\left|{\bf P_{0}}\right|}\;\;\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\,\left|Error_{m}\right|^{2}\,\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{5.31*})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}\,\left|{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\right|}{\left(c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}\,-\,k^{2}\,\eta_{0}\,\lvert{\bf P_{0}}\rvert\right)}\;\left([H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}d^{2\,\alpha-3}\,\left|\log(d)\right|^{2}\,+\,\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,d^{-\frac{9}{7}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This proves (
2.2
) and ends the proof of
Proposition
2.3
.

5.
Quantitative estimates of the
C^{0,\alpha}
-regularity

Here, we show the detailed proof of the
C^{0,\alpha}
- Hölder regularity of the solution to the electromagnetic scattering problem for the effective medium (
1.21
) with
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
being negative definite, which is stated by
Proposition
3.1
. We end this section by proving
Proposition
3.3
.

5.1.
Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

In this subsection, we prove
Proposition
3.1
for the Hölder regularity, up to the boundary, of the solution to the electromagnetic scattering problem (
1.21
) with negative definite effective permeability
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
. The proof shall be divided into the following two steps.

(1)

We show that
E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}},H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
,
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
, excluding the plasmonic eigenvalues.

Remember from
(\ref{compa-bdm})
that we have

(5.1)

\frac{12\;\xi\;c(k)}{\pi\,\left|\pi^{3}\mp 4\,\xi\right|}\,\left|\Omega\right|\,<\,\min\left\{1,\lvert\mathring{\mu_{r}}\rvert,\lvert A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\rvert\right\}.

The tensors
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
and
A_{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
are both characterized by a chosen sign, which allows us to distinguish two cases. In the sequel, we assume that
\xi>2\,\delta\,\pi^{3}
, where
\delta
is a real positive parameter which will be defined later for simplification. It is direct to see from (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) that for
\xi>2\delta\pi^{3}
,
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
is negative definite.

(a)

By taking the lower sign. The inequality
(\ref{compa-bdmp})
becomes:

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\;\frac{8\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}};\;\;\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\right\}.

We denote by
\lambda_{n_{1}}^{(3)}(\Omega)
the closest eigenvalue from the right to
\dfrac{1}{3}
. Set
12
12

12

The accumulation point for the sequence
\{\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is
\dfrac{1}{2}
, so we can infer that
0<\delta_{\star}<\dfrac{1}{6}
.

\delta_{\star}:=\lambda_{n_{1}}^{(3)}(\Omega)-\dfrac{1}{3}
and
\delta:=\left[\dfrac{1}{12\,\delta_{\star}}\right]+1
, where
[\cdot]
is the integer part function. The distribution of the sequence of eigenvalues allows us to distinguish two cases.

(i)

If
0\,<\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,\leq\,\dfrac{1}{3}
, we obtain:

\underset{n}{Inf}\;\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\right)\right|=\pi^{3}.

Hence,
(\ref{compa1})
takes the following form

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|\,<\,\min\left\{1;\;\;\frac{8\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}};\;\;\frac{\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\right\}=\frac{\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}.

Hence,

(5.2)

c(k)<\frac{\pi^{4}}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}.

(ii)

If
\dfrac{1}{3}+\delta_{\star}<\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\leq 1
, we obtain
13
13

13

We have used the fact that

x<1+[x],\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{+}.

:

\pi^{3}-8\,\xi\leq\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1-3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\right)<\pi^{3}\,\left(1-24\,\delta_{\star}\,-\,24\,\delta_{\star}\,\left[\frac{1}{12\,\delta_{\star}}\right]\right)<0.

Consequently,

\underset{n}{Inf}\;\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\right)\right|\,=\,\underset{n}{Inf}\;\left(-\,\pi^{3}\,-\,4\,\xi\,\left(1\,-\,3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\right)\right)=\,-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}.

Hence, from
(\ref{compa1})
, a sufficient condition can be derived as

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\;\frac{8\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}};\;\;\frac{-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}\right\}=\frac{-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}}{4\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}.

Then,

(5.3)

c(k)<\frac{\left(-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}\right)\,\pi}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}.

By combining with the inequalities
(\ref{Ineq1})
and
(\ref{Ineq2})
, we deduce that

(5.4)

c(k)<\min\left\{\frac{\pi^{4}}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|};\frac{\pi\,\left(-\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}\right)}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}\right\}=\frac{\pi^{4}}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}.

(b)

By taking the upper sign. The inequality
(\ref{compa-bdmp})
becomes:

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\;\frac{8\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}};\;\;\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\,\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}\right\},

which can be reduced to

(5.5)

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\;\underset{n}{Inf}\;\;\frac{\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\,\right)\right|}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}\right\},

since

\frac{8\,\xi\,+\,\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}>1.

We denote by
\lambda_{n_{2}}^{(3)}(\Omega)
the closest eigenvalue from the left to
\dfrac{1}{3}
. We set
\delta_{\star}:=\dfrac{1}{3}-\lambda_{n_{2}}^{(3)}(\Omega)
and we set
\delta:=1+\left[\dfrac{1}{12\,\delta_{\star}}\right]
. Two cases shall be studied as follows.

(i)

If
0<\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\leq\dfrac{1}{3}-\delta_{\star}
, we obtain:

\pi^{3}-4\,\xi<\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)-\,1\right)<\pi^{3}\,\left(1-12\,\delta\,\delta_{\star}\right)<0.

Then,

\underset{n}{Inf}\;\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)\right|=\underset{n}{Inf}\;\left(-\,\pi^{3}-4\,\xi\,\left(3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)\right)=-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}.

Hence,
(\ref{FAF1155})
takes the following form

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(4\,\xi\,-\pi^{3}\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\;\frac{-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}\right\}=\frac{-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}},

where, for the last estimation, we have used the fact that
\delta_{\star}\leq\frac{1}{3}
. Then,

(5.6)

c(k)<\frac{\pi\,\left(-\,\pi^{3}+12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}\right)}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}.

(ii)

If
\dfrac{1}{3}<\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\leq 1
, we have

\underset{n}{Inf}\;\left|\pi^{3}+4\,\xi\,\left(3\,\lambda_{n}^{(3)}(\Omega)\,-\,1\right)\right|=\,\pi^{3}.

Then,

\frac{12\,\xi\,c(k)}{\pi\,\left(4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}\right)}\,\left|\Omega\right|<\min\left\{1;\;\frac{\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}\right\}=\frac{\pi^{3}}{4\,\xi\,-\,\pi^{3}}.

Hence,

(5.7)

c(k)\,<\,\frac{\pi^{4}}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}.

By combining with the inequalities
(\ref{Ineq3})
and
(\ref{Ineq4})
, as done in
(\ref{Ineq5})
, we deduce

(5.8)

c(k)\,<\,\min\left\{\frac{\pi\left(-\,\pi^{3}\,+\,12\,\xi\,\delta_{\star}\right)}{12\,\xi\,|\Omega|},\,\frac{\pi^{4}}{12\,\xi\,|\Omega|}\right\}\,<\,\frac{\pi^{4}}{12\,\xi\,\left|\Omega\right|}.

(c)

For the critical case, where
\lambda_{n}^{(3)}=\frac{1}{3}
is an eigenvalue of the Magnetization operator, we can prove that
(\ref{Ineq6})
still holds.

Finally, for both of the lower sign and the upper sign cases, we deduce that under the condition

(5.9)

2\,\delta\,\pi^{3}<\xi<\frac{\pi^{4}}{12\,\left|\Omega\right|\,c(k)},

the operator
{\bf I}\pm\xi\nabla{\bf M}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
, in
(\ref{LS-vec})
, dominates the operator
{\bf K}
and the corresponding coercivity can be attained, which shows that
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
. In a similar way, we can prove that
E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
.

(2)

We prove that
E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}},H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\in C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})
, for
0<\alpha<1
.

We recall, from the electromagnetic scattering problem for the effective medium, that

(5.10)

\begin{cases}\mathrm{Curl}E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}-ik\mathring{\mu_{r}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}=0&\mbox{in }\mathbb{R}^{3}\\
\mathrm{Curl}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}+ik\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}=0&\mbox{in }\mathbb{R}^{3}\\
\nu\times E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega}=\nu\times E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{-}|_{\partial\Omega}\,\,\text{and}\,\,\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega}=\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}|_{\partial\Omega}&\mbox{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

where

\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}={\bf I},\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{3},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathring{\mu_{r}}=\begin{cases}{\bf I}+\dfrac{\eta_{0}k^{2}}{\pm c_{0}}c_{r}^{-3}{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}&\mbox{ in }\Omega,\\
&\\
{\bf I}&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\Omega,\end{cases}

with
H_{+}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
(resp.
E_{+}^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}
) and
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}
(resp.
E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{-}
) denoting the magnetic (resp. electric) fields outside
\Omega
and inside
\Omega
, respectively. It is direct to see that
E_{+}^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}
and
H_{+}^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}
are smooth enough for
\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}=\mathring{\mu_{r}}={\bf I}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\Omega
. Now, let
R>0
, such that
\Omega\subset B(0,R)\subset B(0,2R)
, where
B(0,R)
is the ball centered at the origin with radius
R
. Suppose
\psi_{R}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\Omega)
fulfilling that

\psi_{R}=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{in}\quad B(0,R)\backslash\Omega,\\
&\\
0&\mbox{in}\quad\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash B(0,2R).\end{cases}

Thus it is easy to get that

(5.11)

\psi_{R}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}_{+}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\Omega)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{W}^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\Omega)\quad\mbox{for any}\quad k,\,p>0,\quad\psi_{R}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega}=H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega},

where
\mathbb{W}^{k,p}
is the normal Sobolev space. It suffices to take
k=1
in (
5.11
). Then from the definition of
\psi_{R}
, by using the Trace Theorem for the trace operator
Tr
in a bounded domain with Lipschitz boundary, we have

\lVert Tr(\psi_{R}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+})\rVert_{\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega)}=\lVert H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega}\rVert_{\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega)}\leq c\lVert\psi_{R}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}\rVert_{\mathbb{W}^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\Omega)},

which directly indicates that

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega}\in\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega).

Since
\partial\Omega
is
C^{2}
-regular, it is easy to see that in (
5.10
)

\nu\in C^{1}(\partial\Omega)\subset\mathbb{W}^{1,\infty}(\partial\Omega)\subset\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega),\quad\mbox{for any }p.

Thus, we can obtain that

\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega}\in\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega).

By the boundary transmission condition in (
5.10
), it yields that

(5.12)

\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}|_{\partial\Omega}=\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{+}|_{\partial\Omega}\in\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega)\quad\mbox{for any }p.

Then there holds inside
\Omega
that

(5.13)

\begin{cases}\mathrm{Curl}E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{-}-ik\mathring{\mu_{r}}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}=0&\mbox{in }\Omega,\\
\mathrm{Curl}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}+ik\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{-}=0&\mbox{in }\Omega,\\
\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}|_{\partial\Omega}\,\,\text{and}\,\,\nu\times E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{-}|_{\partial\Omega}\in\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega).\end{cases}

Recall that under the condition
(\ref{cdtxi})
, we have proved that
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
and
E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, which indicates by (
5.13
) that

\mathrm{Curl}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\mathrm{Curl}E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega).

Moreover, by taking advantage of the fact that
\mathring{\mu_{r}}
is constant, we can further derive from (
5.13
) that
\mathrm{div}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}=0
.
Therefore, based on the facts that

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-},\;\mathrm{Curl}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-},\;\mathrm{div}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}|_{\partial\Omega}\in\mathbb{W}^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\Omega),\mbox{ for any }p,

and by utilizing
[
8
, Corollary 5.3]
, with
p=2
, we deduce that

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{W}^{1,2}(\Omega).

By the Sobolev Embedding Theorem, there holds

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{W}^{1,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}(\Omega).

Similarly, from (
5.13
), we have

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-},\;\mathrm{Curl}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-},\;\mathrm{div}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{L}^{6}(\Omega).

Together with (
5.12
), we know that
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{W}^{1,6}(\Omega)
by using
[
8
, Corollary 5.3]
again. Thus, it is easy to get

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{W}^{1,6}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow C^{0,\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)\subset\mathbb{L}^{p}(\Omega),\quad\mbox{for any }p,

for the bounded domain
\Omega
, by the Sobolev Embedding Theorem, which further indicates by (
5.13
) that

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-},\;\mathrm{Curl}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-},\;\mathrm{div}H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{L}^{p}(\Omega),\quad\mbox{for any }p.

Combining with (
5.12
) and the Sobolev Embedding Theorem, we can deduce that

H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-}\in\mathbb{W}^{1,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow C^{0,\alpha}(\Omega)\quad\mbox{for any }p>3\mbox{ and }0<\alpha<1,

up to the boundary. With a similar argument, we can derive the result that
H^{\mathring{\mu_{r}}}_{-},E^{\mathring{\epsilon_{r}}}_{-}\in C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})
for any
0<\alpha<1
. This ends the proof of
Proposition
3.1
.

5.2.
Proof of
Proposition
3.3

In
Proposition
3.1
, we have shown the qualitative
C^{0,\alpha}
-regularity property. Here, we provide quantitative estimates of this
C^{0,\alpha}
-regularity in terms of the parameters
k
and
\beta
(and
a
therefore). To justify
(\ref{ToproveinAppendix})
we start by recalling the L.S.E given by
(\ref{LS-1})
,

\displaystyle H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}(x)\,-\,\frac{\eta_{0}\,k^{2}}{\pm\,c_{0}\,c_{r}^{3}}\,\left[-\,\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(x)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\left({\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\cdot H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(x)\right]\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}(x)\,-\,\pm\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,\left[-\,\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(x)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(x)\right]\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}(x,\theta).

Based on
(\ref{IBA})
and
(\ref{Equa0758})
, we choose the lower sign to obtain

(5.14)

\displaystyle H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,+\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,\left[-\,\nabla{\bf M^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,+\,k^{2}\,{\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right]\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,H^{Inc}

\displaystyle H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,+\,\frac{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}{\left(1\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}\,Curl^{2}{\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{i\,k}{\left(1\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}\,H^{Inc}

(5.15)

\displaystyle H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,-\,\frac{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}{\left(1\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}\,Curl\,SL^{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{i\,k}{\left(1\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}\,H^{Inc}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}{\left(1\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}\,Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right),\quad\text{in}\quad\Omega,

where
SL^{k}(\cdot)
is the vectorial Single Layer operator defined by

SL^{k}(E)(x)\,:=\,\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,E(y)\,d\sigma(y),\quad x\in\Omega.

And, by going to the boundary and using the fact that

(5.16)

Curl\,SL^{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,=\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,+\,\frac{1}{2}\,\nu\,\times\,\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right),\quad\text{on}\;\partial\Omega,

where

(5.17)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(x)\,:=\,p.v.\,\int_{\partial\Omega}\underset{x}{\nabla}\Phi_{k}(x,y)\,\times\,\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(y)\,d\sigma(y),\quad x\in\partial\Omega,

we obtain

(5.18)

\frac{\left(2\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}{2\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,-\,\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,=\,\frac{i\,k}{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\nu\times H^{Inc}\,-\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right).

Then,

\displaystyle\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left[\frac{\left(2\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}{2\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,-\,\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right]^{-1}\,\Bigg[\nu\times\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)+\frac{i\,k}{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\nu\times H^{Inc}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\Bigg].

Then, by taking the
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}
-norm in both sides, we obtain

(5.19)

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\text{dist}}\;\Bigg[\left\|\nu\times\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,\frac{k}{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left\|\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\Bigg],

where

(5.20)

\text{dist}:=\text{dist}\left[\frac{\left(2\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}{2\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,;\,\sigma\left(\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\right]\overset{(\ref{exp-T-neg})}{=}\text{dist}\left[\frac{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,2\,\xi\right)}{12\,\xi}\,\,;\,\sigma\left(\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\right].

To continue with the estimation of
dist
, we need to investigate the spectrum of the operator
\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}
denoted by
\sigma\left(\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)
. The coming lemma gives more clarification on this.

Lemma 5.1
.

The following relation holds,

(5.21)

\sigma\left(\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\;=\;\sigma\left(\nabla{\bf M}\right)\;-\;\frac{1}{2},

where
\nabla{\bf M}
is the Magnetization operator and
\sigma\left(\nabla{\bf M}\right)
is its spectrum.

Proof.

Let
\lambda_{n}\,\in\,\sigma\left(\nabla{\bf M}\right)
, i.e.
\exists\,V_{n}\,\in\,\nabla
-Harmonic such that

\displaystyle\nabla{\bf M}\left(V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\lambda_{n}\,V_{n},\quad\text{in}\;\Omega.

\displaystyle-\,\nabla\,\mathrm{div}\,{\bf N}\left(V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\lambda_{n}\,V_{n},\quad\text{in}\;\Omega.

\displaystyle Curl^{2}\,{\bf N}\left(V_{n}\right)\,+\,\Delta\,{\bf N}\left(V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\;\lambda_{n}\,V_{n},\quad\text{in}\;\Omega.

\displaystyle Curl^{2}\,{\bf N}\left(V_{n}\right)\,-\,V_{n}\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\;\lambda_{n}\,V_{n},\quad\text{in}\;\Omega.

\displaystyle Curl^{2}\,{\bf N}\left(V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(1\,-\,\lambda_{n}\right)\,V_{n},\quad\text{in}\;\Omega.

\displaystyle Curl\,\left[{\bf N}\left(CurlV_{n}\right)\,-\,SL\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\right]\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(1\,-\,\lambda_{n}\right)\,V_{n},\quad\text{in}\;\Omega.

\displaystyle Curl\,SL\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(-\,1\,+\,\lambda_{n}\right)\,V_{n},\quad\text{in}\;\Omega.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\,+\,\frac{1}{2}\,\nu\,\times\,\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{CurlSl})}{=}

\displaystyle\,\left(-\,1\,+\,\lambda_{n}\right)\,V_{n},\quad\text{on}\;\partial\Omega.

\displaystyle\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\,+\,\frac{1}{2}\,\nu\times\,\left[\nu\,\times\,\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\right]\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(-\,1\,+\,\lambda_{n}\right)\,\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right),\quad\text{on}\;\partial\Omega.

\displaystyle\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\,-\,\frac{1}{2}\,\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(-\,1\,+\,\lambda_{n}\right)\,\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right),\quad\text{on}\;\partial\Omega.

\displaystyle\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right)\;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(-\,\frac{1}{2}\,+\,\lambda_{n}\right)\,\left(\nu\times V_{n}\right),\quad\text{on}\;\partial\Omega.

This concludes the proof of the announced Lemma.
∎

Using
(\ref{ShiftSpectrum})
, we rewrite
(\ref{distbLemma})
as

\displaystyle\text{dist}\,=\,\text{dist}\left[\frac{\left(\pi^{3}\,-\,2\,\xi\right)}{12\,\xi}\,\,;\,\sigma\left(\nabla{\bf M}\right)\,-\,\frac{1}{2}\right]\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\text{dist}\left[\frac{1}{3}\,+\,\frac{\pi^{3}}{12\,\xi}\,\,;\,\sigma\left(\nabla{\bf M}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{xn0=...+betan0})}{=}

\displaystyle\,\text{dist}\left[\dfrac{\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,+\,\beta\,\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,\dfrac{1}{3}\right)}{1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}\,;\,\sigma\left(\nabla{\bf M}\right)\right].

As
\beta
is taken to be small, the closest eigenvalue to

\dfrac{\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,+\,\beta\,\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,\dfrac{1}{3}\right)}{1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)},

is given by
\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)
. Hence,

(5.22)

\text{dist}\;=\;\text{dist}\left[\dfrac{\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,+\,\beta\,\left(\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,\dfrac{1}{3}\right)}{1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}\,;\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\right]=\,\frac{\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}{3\,\left[1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)\right]}\,=\,\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\beta\right|\right).

Then, by going back to
(\ref{Eqbdist})
, and using
(\ref{Estdist})
, we derive that

(5.23)

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\,\left[1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)\right]}{\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}\;\Bigg[\left\|\nu\times\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{k}{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\Bigg].

We need to estimate the first term on the R.H.S. To do this, from
(\ref{DefAk})
, we have

\displaystyle\nu\times\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\,-\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(x)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\nu\times\int_{\partial\Omega}\underset{x}{\nabla}\left(\Phi_{k}\,-\,\Phi_{0}\right)(x,y)\,\times\,\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(y)\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{k^{2}}{8\,\pi}\,\nu\times\int_{\partial\Omega}\underset{x}{\nabla}\left|x-y\right|\,\times\,\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(y)\,d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\,\pi}\,\nu\times\int_{\partial\Omega}\left(\sum_{j\geq 3}\frac{(i\,k)^{j}}{j!}\underset{x}{\nabla}\left|x-y\right|^{j-1}\right)\times\,\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)(y)\,d\sigma(y).

Next, we set
\beth
to be the operator defined by

\displaystyle\beth\;:\;\mathbb{L}^{2}\left(\partial\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{2}\left(\partial\Omega\right)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\beth\left(E\right)(\cdot):=\,\nu\times\int_{\partial\Omega}\nabla\left|\cdot-y\right|\,\times\,E(y)\,d\sigma(y).

Thus,

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\,-\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{8\,\pi}\,\left\|\beth\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)\right)}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle k^{3}\,C_{1}(\Omega,k)\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)},

where

C_{1}(\Omega,k)\,=\,\frac{1}{4\ \pi}\,\left[\int_{\partial\Omega}\,\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\sum_{j\geq 3}\frac{i^{j}\,k^{j-3}}{j!}\nabla\left|x-y\right|^{j-1}\right|^{2}\,d\sigma(y)\,d\sigma(x)\right],

which is of order one with respect to the parameter
k
. In addition, we have the following estimation

\displaystyle\left\|\beth\left(E\right)\right\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nu(x)\times\int_{\partial\Omega}\nabla\left|x-y\right|\,\times\,E(y)\,d\sigma(y)\,\right|^{2}\,d\sigma(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\int_{\partial\Omega}\nabla\left|x-y\right|\,\times\,E(y)\,d\sigma(y)\,\right|^{2}\,d\sigma(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nabla\left|x-y\right|\,\right|^{2}\,d\sigma(y)\,d\sigma(x)\;\left\|E\right\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}=\,\left|\partial\Omega\right|^{2}\;\left\|E\right\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}.

Then,

\left\|\beth\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)\right)}\,\leq\,\left|\partial\Omega\right|,

and this implies,

(5.24)

\left\|\nu\times\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\,-\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{0}\right)\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{8\,\pi}\,\left|\partial\Omega\right|\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k^{3}\,C_{1}(\Omega,k)\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}.

Next, by using
(\ref{Eq1237})
, the inequality
(\ref{EqB13})
becomes

(5.25)

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\,\left[1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)\right]}{\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}\;\Bigg[\frac{k^{2}}{8\,\pi}\,\left|\partial\Omega\right|\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k^{3}\,C_{1}(\Omega,k)\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{k}{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\Bigg].

Using the fact that

k^{2}\,\leq\,\frac{\pi\,\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}{3\,\left|\Omega\right|},

see
(\ref{WBMX})
and
(\ref{kscalebeta})
, we rewrite the previous inequality
(\ref{bk2})
as

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{\left|\partial\Omega\right|}{8\,\left|\Omega\right|}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\,\pi\,k\,C_{1}(\Omega,k)\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)}{\left|\Omega\right|}\,\Bigg[\,\frac{1}{8}\,\left|\partial\Omega\right|\,+\,\pi\,k\,C_{1}(\Omega,k)\Bigg]\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{3\,\left[1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)\right]}{\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}\;\frac{k}{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{3\,\left[1\,+\,\beta\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)\right]}{\left|\beta\right|\,\left(3\,\lambda_{m_{0}}^{(3)}\left(\Omega\right)\,-\,1\right)^{2}}\;\left\|\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)},

which, under the condition

(5.26)

\frac{\left|\partial\Omega\right|}{8\,\left|\Omega\right|}\,<\,1,

the smallness of
k
and
\beta
and the fact that
\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,\sim\,1
, gives us

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)\right)}\;\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Now, using the fact that

\left\|\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)\right)}\;\sim\;1,

we reduce the previous estimation to

(5.27)

\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,\lesssim\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\,\frac{1}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

We need to estimate
\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
. To do this, by recalling
(\ref{EqB6})
and taking the Curl operator in its both sides, we obtain

\displaystyle Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,i\,k\,Curl\left(H^{Inc}\right)\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,k^{2}\,Curl\left({\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)

(5.28)

\displaystyle Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{IncWave})}{=}

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,E^{Inc}\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,k^{2}\,Curl\left({\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|Curl{\bf N^{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}\left(\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)\right)}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{es-coro1})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

As
\beta\sim k^{2}
, with
k
taken to be small, and both
\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
and

are of order one with respect to the parameters

and
\beta
, we reduce the previous estimation into the following one

\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim\,\,\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Plugging the previous estimation into
(\ref{DH})
, we deduce that

(5.29)

\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,\lesssim\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\;\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

From
(\ref{EqAS})
, we recall that

\displaystyle\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{2\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}{\left(2\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)}\,\left[\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,+\,\frac{i\,k}{\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}}\,\nu\times H^{Inc}\,-\,\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}.

In addition, by using the continuity of the operator
\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}
from

\mathbb{L}^{2}_{t}(\partial\Omega)
to
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{t}(\partial\Omega)
, see
[

, where we recall that

\mathbb{H}_{t}^{m}(\partial\Omega)\,:=\,\left\{E:\partial\Omega\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{3};\;E\in\mathbb{H}^{m}(\partial\Omega);\,\nu\cdot E\,=\,0\;\text{on}\;\partial\Omega\right\},\quad m\in\mathbb{N},

we obtain

\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,\lesssim\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}+\,\left\|\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}.

Additionally, the operator

to

. Then,

(5.30)

\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,\lesssim\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}+\,\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}.

The estimation of

is needed. To achieve this, we recall from
(\ref{Eq0957109})
that

(5.31)

\displaystyle Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,E^{Inc}\,-\,\xi\,{\bf T}^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\,k^{2}\,Curl\left({\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|Curl\left({\bf N^{k}}\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}.

Furthermore, as the operator
Curl{\bf N^{k}}
is continuous from
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)
to
\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)\,\subset\,\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}\left(\Omega\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{es-coro1})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}\,+\,\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

By plugging the previous equation into
(\ref{EqbEstCurlH})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}+\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}\,+\,\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{EqB22})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\;\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}+\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Omega\right)}.

Knowing that
\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)},\;\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}\left(\partial\Omega\right)}

, and by taking into account the fact that

, we reduce the previous inequality into

Furthermore, by Sobolev embeddings, see
[
14
, Theorem 9.16]
, we deduce that

, with

. Hence,

Going back to
(\ref{Eq0259})
and taking the
