-norm in its both sides, we get

\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{r}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim\,\left\|Curl\,SL^{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{r}(\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{r}(\Omega)}\,+\,\left\|Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{r}(\Omega)},

Using

As the parameter

(5.34)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{ASEq1012})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\;\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)},\quad r\geq 2.

In addition, by plugging the previous estimation into

, we derive

which gives us an estimation of

in

, for

. At this stage we use

, we get

.

(1)

Estimation of

\displaystyle+

And, using the continuity of the operator

from

, we obtain

-regularity

of

, the operator

Consequently, based on

Knowing that

(5.38)

Regarding the previous estimation, as
(\ref{HZ18461012})
, to finish with the estimation of

, we need to estimate

. To achieve this, we go back to

to obtain

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}\left(Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}.

And, using the continuity of the operator
\nu\times Curl{\bf N^{k}}
from

to
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|Curl\left(H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{EBEq})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|E^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\ \,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}\left(\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\ \,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Omega)}.

Thanks to
[
26
, Theorem 4.3]
, we know that the operator
\nu\times\widetilde{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}
is continuous from
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)
to
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nu\times H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}\,+\,k\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\partial\Omega)}+k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{EqB22})}{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\;\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,k^{2}\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Omega)}.

Going back to
(\ref{SSI})
and using the previous estimation we end up with the following estimation

\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim\,\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\;\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}+\;\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Moreover, thanks to
(\ref{Eq19141012})
, we obtain

\left\|H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim\,\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}.

In addition, the following embedding result holds

\mathbb{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Omega)\;\hookrightarrow\;C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega}),\quad\text{with}\quad 0\,<\,\alpha\,<\,1,

see
[
1
, Section 4.12]
, and this implies

\left[H^{\mathring{\mu}_{r}}\right]_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\;\lesssim\;\frac{k^{3}}{\left|\beta\right|^{2}}\,\left\|H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,+\,\frac{k}{\left|\beta\right|}\,\left\|\nu\times H^{Inc}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\partial\Omega)},\quad\text{with}\quad 0\,<\,\alpha\,<\,1.

This ends the proof of
Proposition
3.3
.

6.
Appendix: Counting Lemmas

Two lemmas are used in the proof of our main theorems to calculate the sums of inverse distances between nano-particles inside
\Omega
and near
\partial\Omega
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose
{z}_{m}
,
m=1,\cdots,\aleph
, are defined in
(
1.4
)
. For
k>0
, we have the following formula:

\sum_{j=1\atop j\neq m}^{\aleph}|z_{j}-z_{m}|^{-k}=\begin{cases}{\mathcal{O}}\left(d^{-3}\right),&\text{\hskip 10.22217ptif $k<3$,}\\
&\\
{\mathcal{O}}\left(d^{-3}\left(1\,+\,\left|\log(d)\right|\right)\right),&\text{\hskip 10.22217ptif $k=3$,}\\
&\\
{\mathcal{O}}\left(d^{-k}\right),&\text{\hskip 10.22217ptif $k>3$.}\end{cases}

Lemma
6.1
provides us with a straightforward way to count the number of the particles and evaluate the distance between any two points
z_{j}\in D_{j}
and
z_{m}\in D_{m}
,
we refer to
[
3
, Sectoin 3.3]
for more details.

The following lemma is concerned with the counting of the particles which are very close to the boundary
\partial\Omega
. Let
z_{m}
be an arbitrary fixed position (of an arbitrary particle). From
z_{m}
, we split the space into
d
-equidistant cubes
\Sigma_{\ell}
, with
F_{\ell}
being their corresponding surfaces in a fixed direction such that
F_{\ell}
support some of the points
(z_{\ell})_{\ell=1,\ell\neq m}^{\aleph}
. We know, from
[
5
, Lemma A.1]
, that the number of such cubes
\Sigma_{\ell}
is at most of order
{\mathcal{O}}(\aleph^{\frac{1}{3}})
. Then, for the particles counting in the area
\Omega\,\setminus\,\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}
, we can first assume that any small part of
\partial\Omega
is flat under the condition that
\partial\Omega
is
C^{1,\alpha}
-regular and
a
is sufficiently small. Next, we divide the region in
\Omega\,\setminus\,\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}
near the considered flat part into concentric layers such that there are at most
(2n+1)^{2}
small cubes
\Omega_{n}
, with
n=1,\cdots,\left[\aleph^{\frac{1}{3}}\right]
, up to the
n
-th layer intersecting with the surface. It is obvious that the number of the particles located in the
n
-th layer is at most of order
{\mathcal{O}}((2n+1)^{2}-(2n-1)^{2})
and the distance from any small cube
\Omega_{n}
containing the particles to
z_{m}
is at least
(n+\ell)d
. Thus we have the following counting lemma presenting an upper bound with respect to the particles distances.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any fixed
z_{m}
,
m=1,\cdots,\aleph
, the following formula holds,

(6.1)

\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left(\int_{\Omega\setminus\underset{m=1}{\overset{\aleph}{\cup}}\Omega_{m}}\;\frac{1}{|z_{m}-z|^{3}}\,dz\right)^{2}={\mathcal{O}}\left(d^{-1}\right).

Proof.

It is clear from the distribution described above
Lemma
6.2
that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varkappa}\,

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{m=1}^{\aleph}\left(\int_{\Omega\backslash\cup_{m=1}^{\aleph}\Omega_{m}}\frac{1}{|z_{m}-z|^{3}}\,dz\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{\aleph^{\frac{1}{3}}}\sum_{z_{\ell}\in F_{\ell}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\aleph^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{\left|\Omega_{n}\right|\,\left[(2n+1)^{2}\,-\,(2n-1)^{2}\right]}{(n+\ell)^{3}d^{3}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\sum_{\ell=1}^{\aleph^{\frac{1}{3}}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\aleph^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{n\ell}{(n+\ell)^{3}}\right)^{2}.

By referring to the precise computations derived in
[
16
, Section 4, formula (4.35)]
, we get

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varkappa}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(3\,\bar{c}\,+\,\frac{9}{2}\right)\,d^{-1}\,+\,\frac{2\,(3\,d^{-2}\,-\,d^{-1}\,-\,2)}{1\,+\,d^{-1}}\,-\,6\,d^{-1}\,\log\left(\frac{2\,d^{-1}}{1\,+\,d^{-1}}\right)+\frac{3\,d\,(1-d)}{(1\,+\,d^{3})(1\,+\,d^{2})},

where
\bar{c}
is a positive constant and
d
is given by
(\ref{basic-ad})
. Then
(\ref{count-bound})
can be obtained as a direct consequence of the fact that
d
is a small parameter.
∎
